- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical (a genuine, live security hole — the exact class of problem this whole session's emergency-gate work was meant to close, just missed because these routes live in a different file with a different middleware group)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 21:35, batch 24 of the permission rollout, while investigating `oldEnquiryCrudController`/`UserImportExportController` reachability and noticing the latter's routes live in `routes/web.php`, not any `routes/backpack/*.php` file.
- **Fixed:** 20-09-2026 21:45 — see `ai-changelogs-20-09-2026.md`, batch 24 entry.
- **Where:** `routes/web.php` — the `Route::prefix('admin/users')->group(...)` block was wrapped in `Route::middleware(['auth', 'verified'])`, not the `admin` middleware group (`[CheckIfAdmin::class, ConvertEmptyStringsToNull::class, AuthenticateSession::class]`) that every route in `routes/backpack/*.php` uses.
- **Description:** Every other admin route in this application goes through `routes/backpack/*.php`'s route group, which applies `array_merge((array) config('backpack.base.web_middleware', 'web'), (array) config('backpack.base.middleware_key', 'admin'))` — the `admin` key resolving to `CheckIfAdmin` (this session's emergency `user_type === 'Emp' && is_active` gate), among others. `UserImportExportController`'s routes, defined separately in `routes/web.php`, used a hand-written `['auth', 'verified']` middleware list instead — `auth` just checks the user is logged in (any guard/any user type), `verified` just checks email verification. **Neither check has anything to do with staff-vs-customer status or Spatie permissions.** Combined with `UserImportExportController` itself having zero internal permission checks (see BUG-005-style precedent — this controller was apparently never touched by the earlier emergency-gate pass, since it isn't reached by `routes/backpack/*.php`'s glob-loaded files at all), this meant **any authenticated, email-verified user of any type — potentially including customers/DSAs, not just staff — could reach bulk user import/export**, a highly sensitive operation (mass user creation, data export). Confirmed live, before the fix: a non-`Emp` (`Associate`) user's request to `GET /admin/users/import` succeeded in reaching the controller (status was whatever the controller itself returned, not a `403`); after the fix, the same request correctly returns `403` from `CheckIfAdmin`.
- **Fix applied:** replaced `Route::middleware(['auth', 'verified'])` with the exact same `array_merge(...)` construct used by `routes/backpack/core.php`'s own group, so this route group now goes through the identical `web` + `admin` (`CheckIfAdmin`) middleware chain as every other admin route in the app. Also added explicit Spatie permission checks (`users.import`/`users.export`, newly minted) inside every one of the controller's 7 public methods, matching this rollout's standard pattern, and moved the controller into `Admin\Org\User` namespace alongside `UserCrudController` for consistency and discoverability.

### BUG-042 — `UserImportExportController::export()` instantiates a class it never imports, resolving to the wrong namespace

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** High (fatal, uncaught `Error` — not caught by the method's own `catch (Exception $e)`, since PHP's `Error` for "class not found" is not an `Exception` subclass — every call to `export()` would have crashed hard)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 21:35, batch 24 of the permission rollout, while reading the controller before wiring — noticed `export()` calls `new UserExporter` with no corresponding `use` statement anywhere in the file.
- **Fixed:** 20-09-2026 21:40 — see `ai-changelogs-20-09-2026.md`, batch 24 entry.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Org/User/UserImportExportController.php` (pre-move: `app/Http/Controllers/UserImportExportController.php`), `export()`.
- **Description:** With no `use` import, `new UserExporter` resolves relative to the file's own namespace (`App\Http\Controllers`, pre-move) — i.e. `App\Http\Controllers\UserExporter`, which does not exist (confirmed via `class_exists()`). The real class is `App\Services\Exporters\UserExporter` (confirmed via `find app -iname "UserExporter.php"`) — a sibling to the already-correctly-imported `App\Services\Importers\UserImporter` used by `import()`. This looks like a simple missing-import oversight, not a deeper design issue.
- **Fix applied:** added `use App\Services\Exporters\UserExporter;`. Trivial, unambiguous (only one class by that name exists in the entire codebase), required to get past instantiation to test `export()` at all.

### BUG-043 — `UserImportExportController`'s views do not exist anywhere

- **Status:** OPEN
- **Severity:** High (every one of the controller's 4 view-rendering actions 500s with "View not found," even past BUG-041's gate fix and with the correct permission granted — the feature has never been able to render anything)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 21:50, batch 24 of the permission rollout, while verifying the `users.import` permission gate end-to-end (`GET /admin/users/import` with the correct permission granted still returned `500`).
- **Modified:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — re-verified: the views were never built; the import/export screens still 500. Needs a decision on whether the feature is wanted.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Org/User/UserImportExportController.php` — `showImportForm()` (`admin.users.import`), `showExportForm()` (`admin.users.export`), `importHistory()` (`admin.users.import-history`), `exportHistory()` (`admin.users.export-history`). None of the four exist anywhere under `resources/views/` — confirmed via `find resources/views/admin -maxdepth 1 -iname "*user*"`, which found only the unrelated `resources/views/admin/user-type/` directory; no `resources/views/admin/users/` directory exists at all.
- **Description:** Even with BUG-041 (gate bypass) and BUG-042 (missing import) fixed, and the correct `users.import` permission granted, `GET /admin/users/import` still returns `500: View [admin.users.import] not found.` This is the third independent bug stacked on this single controller — the underlying feature (bulk user import/export via Excel) has apparently never had its UI built, or the views were deleted/moved without the controller being updated to match, at some point in this controller's history.
- **Proposed solution:** needs the actual view templates built (or, if they exist under different names/paths not yet found, the controller's `view(...)` calls corrected to point at them). Not fixed in this rollout — building 4 new Blade views (import form, export form, import history table, export history table) is a real feature-completion task, not a permission-gating fix. The security fix (BUG-041) and the trivial import fix (BUG-042) stand on their own regardless of this remaining gap — closing the security hole was the priority, and is done; the feature being otherwise non-functional was already effectively true before this batch (it 500'd before too, just via a different, less severe symptom sequence).

### BUG-044 — `ExportController` and `PerformanceController` had zero authentication of any kind — fully public to anonymous visitors

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical (unauthenticated exposure — no login of any kind was needed to reach these routes, the most severe possible form of this session's core "missing gate" problem)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 22:00, immediately after fixing BUG-041, while doing a quick broader sweep of the rest of `routes/web.php` for the same gate-bypass pattern (given how serious BUG-041 turned out to be, worth checking the whole file rather than assuming it was the only instance).
- **Fixed:** 20-09-2026 22:10 — see `ai-changelogs-20-09-2026.md`, batch 24 entry.
- **Where:** `routes/web.php` — `Route::get('/performance-report', ...)` and the `Route::prefix('export')->group(...)` block (3 routes: `vehicle-data`, `vehicle-data-csv`, `vehicle-data-simple-csv`) had **no middleware wrapper at all** beyond Laravel's own default `web` group (session/CSRF/cookies) — no `auth`, no `verified`, no `admin`/`CheckIfAdmin`, nothing. `app/Http/Controllers/ExportController.php` (all 3 methods) and `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/PerformanceController.php::report()` had no internal permission checks either.
- **Description:** Confirmed via `php artisan route:list --path=export -v` / `--path=performance-report -v` (before fixing) that these routes carried only the `web` middleware. This means, prior to this fix, **any anonymous visitor with no account at all** could download the full vehicle data export (Excel/CSV) or view sales performance analytics, simply by knowing the URL. This is the same class of problem as BUG-041, one level more severe (BUG-041 at least required a real, verified login; this required nothing). `PerformanceController::report()` calls `auth()->user()->aggregatePerformance(...)` — for a true anonymous guest this would have thrown a fatal error calling a method on `null` rather than leaking data, but any authenticated user of *any* type (customer, DSA, anyone) could reach it and get real performance data, same as `ExportController`'s exposure. Separately (and unrelated to the security fix): `app/Exports/VehicleDataExport` (the class all three `ExportController` methods instantiate) **does not exist anywhere in the codebase** (confirmed via `find app -iname "VehicleDataExport.php"` — zero results) — so even with the gate now correctly closed, these 3 export methods still fatal when actually invoked by an authorized user. Not fixed, and not counted as part of BUG-044 — noted in the controller's own docblock as a separate, pre-existing gap.
- **Fix applied:** wrapped both the `performance-report` route and the `export` prefix group in the same `array_merge((array) config('backpack.base.web_middleware', 'web'), (array) config('backpack.base.middleware_key', 'admin'))` construct used everywhere else in this rollout, putting all 4 routes through the standard `web` + `admin` (`CheckIfAdmin`) chain. Added `backpack_user()->can('vehicles.view')` checks to all 3 `ExportController` methods (reusing the existing, adjacent `vehicles.view` permission — read-only export of the same vehicle domain data already gated there, not a new resource) and minted a new `performance.view` permission (id 105) for `PerformanceController::report()`, since no existing permission covered that domain. Verified live: an anonymous guest request to either route now correctly redirects (`302`) to login instead of succeeding; an authenticated staff user without the permission gets `403`.

### BUG-045 — `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/oldEnquiryCrudController.php` is entirely unreachable — no route anywhere references it

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — no action, per the same standing decision as BUG-024's 20 dead controllers)
- **Severity:** Low (dead code, zero live exposure)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 22:20, while scoping the next batch per `TASK_STATE.md` section 6 (which flagged this file as "check reachability first, name suggests it may be superseded/dead").
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/oldEnquiryCrudController.php` (class `EnquiryCrudController`, same class name as the real, live `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/EnquiryCrudController.php` — different file, same namespace `App\Http\Controllers\Admin`, so both files declaring the identical FQCN would fatal with a redeclaration error if both were ever autoloaded from the same PSR-4 path; only the real one is actually loaded because Composer's classmap/PSR-4 resolves `EnquiryCrudController` to the correctly-cased filename).
- **Description:** `grep -rn "oldEnquiryCrudController" routes/ app/` returns zero matches — nothing constructs or routes to this class anywhere in the codebase. `php artisan route:list` confirms every `admin/enquir*` and `admin/enquiry/*` route resolves to the real `EnquiryCrudController`, none to this file. This is almost certainly a superseded draft/backup left in place when `EnquiryCrudController` was rewritten, matching the same dead-file pattern as BUG-024's 20 controllers.
- **Proposed solution:** No action for this rollout, per the user's earlier explicit decision to document BUG-024-pattern dead controllers rather than delete or migrate them. Deletion (once confirmed safe with the user/repo owner) would remove a duplicate-class-name hazard, but that's a separate cleanup decision outside this rollout's scope.

### BUG-046 — `EnquiryCrudController` has 5 registered routes pointing at methods that don't exist on the class

- **Status:** OPEN
- **Severity:** High (each of these 5 routes fatals with a `BadMethodCallException`/500 the instant it's hit — not caught by any `try/catch`, since Laravel's own controller dispatcher throws before the method body would even run)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 00:10, during batch 26 (permission rollout for `EnquiryCrudController`), while cross-referencing every route registered against `EnquiryCrudController` in `routes/backpack/core.php`/`booking.php` against the controller's actual `public function` list (`grep -n "public function " EnquiryCrudController.php`) before deciding where to place permission checks.
- **Where:** `routes/backpack/core.php` — `Route::get('enquiries/pending', [...,'pendingList'])` (route name `enquiry.pending`), `Route::get('enquiries/erroneous', [...,'erroneousList'])` (`enquiry.erroneous`), `Route::match(['get','post'], 'enquiry/data', [...,'gridData'])` (`enquiry.data`), `Route::get('enquiry/export', [...,'exportData'])` (`enquiry.export`), `Route::get('enquiry/sales-consultants', [...,'getSalesConsultants'])`.
- **Description:** None of `pendingList`, `erroneousList`, `gridData`, `exportData`, or `getSalesConsultants` are defined anywhere in `EnquiryCrudController` (confirmed via `grep -n "function pendingList\|function erroneousList\|function gridData\|function exportData\|function getSalesConsultants"` — zero matches) or in any trait it uses (`CreateOperation`/`DeleteOperation`/`ListOperation`/`UpdateOperation` don't provide these names either). Since these are registered via the `[Controller::class, 'method']` array callable syntax rather than string `'Controller@method'`, Laravel resolves the callable at dispatch time and throws `Error: Call to undefined method` — not the softer `BadMethodCallException` that `Illuminate\Routing\Controller::__call()` produces for string-based dispatch, and definitely not something any `catch (\Exception $e)` block in this class could intercept (it's a `\Error`, not an `\Exception`, and it happens before the controller method is ever invoked). Two of these route names collide confusingly with real, working sibling routes: `enquiry/data` (POST, broken `gridData`) vs. the separate, working `enquiries/data` (POST, real `data()` method); `enquiry/export` (GET, broken `exportData`) vs. the separate, working `enquiries/export` (GET, real `export()` method) — almost certainly the source of the bug: whoever wired these routes typed the wrong method name for a near-duplicate URL, likely intending them to call the real `data()`/`export()` methods.
- **Proposed solution:** Most likely fix is to point `gridData`→`data` and `exportData`→`export` in the route definitions (probably what was intended, given the naming collision), and either implement `pendingList`/`erroneousList`/`getSalesConsultants` or remove those 3 routes if the corresponding UI features were abandoned. Not fixed in this rollout — implementing missing business logic or guessing intent behind broken routes is outside a permission-gating pass's scope; only permission checks were added to genuinely-reachable methods (this batch's `SLS_ENQR_VIEW` gate was still applied to `pendingList`/`erroneousList`/`getSalesConsultants` in the route-to-permission mapping documentation for when someone does implement them, but no method body exists yet to actually gate).

### BUG-047 — `EnquiryCrudController::destroy()`/`search()`/`showDetailsRow()` had no permission enforcement at all before this batch

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical (same class as this whole rollout's premise — any authenticated backpack user, regardless of assigned permissions, could delete any enquiry or pull the full paginated enquiry grid via the DataTables AJAX endpoint)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 00:15, during batch 26, after adding inline `backpack_user()->can(...)` checks to all 43 of the controller's own action methods and then checking which public actions were actually reachable that *weren't* touched — `destroy`, `search`, and `showDetailsRow` are not overridden in the class at all, so they run Backpack's `DeleteOperation`/`ListOperation` trait defaults verbatim.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Enquiry/EnquiryCrudController.php` — trait defaults from `vendor/backpack/crud/src/app/Http/Controllers/Operations/DeleteOperation.php::destroy()` and `ListOperation.php::search()`/`showDetailsRow()`.
- **Description:** All three trait-default methods gate only via `$this->crud->hasAccessOrFail('delete'|'list')` — Backpack's own internal CRUD-ability system, not this app's Spatie permission system. Critically, `DeleteOperation::setupDeleteDefaults()` and `ListOperation::setupListDefaults()` both call `$this->crud->allowAccess(...)` unconditionally as part of their own bootstrap (confirmed by reading the trait source), meaning `hasAccessOrFail()` passes for **every** authenticated backpack user by default — there was no code path denying access to any of the three. This is the same root cause pattern as BUG-007 (`hasAccessOrFail` not being a real gate on its own) and the same *consequence* as BUG-040 (an unprotected mutation) plus a previously-undocumented unprotected read (`search`/`showDetailsRow`, the actual DataTables AJAX grid data).
- **Fix applied:** overrode all three methods explicitly. `destroy($id)` now checks `backpack_user()->can('SLS_ENQR_DELETE')` before replicating the trait's original body (`hasAccessOrFail('delete')` → resolve current entry ID → `$this->crud->delete($id)`) so behavior is otherwise unchanged. `search()`/`showDetailsRow($id)` use PHP trait conflict-resolution aliasing (`ListOperation::search as protected traitSearch;` / `::showDetailsRow as protected traitShowDetailsRow;`) so the original ~50-line DataTables logic isn't duplicated/forked — the new public methods just check `backpack_user()->can('SLS_ENQR_VIEW')` then delegate to the aliased trait method. Verified live via HTTP-kernel round-trip in a rolled-back transaction: `DELETE /admin/enquiry/999999` → `403` with no permission, `404` (real not-found, past the gate) with `SLS_ENQR_DELETE` granted; `POST /admin/enquiry/search` → `403` with no permission (not exercised past the gate — real DataTables query against live data is heavy, unrelated pre-existing logic, and OOM'd a test tinker process when attempted, so only the negative case was verified end-to-end for `search`).

### BUG-048 — `QuotationCrudController` — `quotation-form/pending` route has no matching `pendingQuotations` method

- **Status:** OPEN
- **Severity:** High (fatals with `Error: Call to undefined method` the instant it's hit — same failure mode as BUG-046)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 01:00, during batch 27 (`QuotationCrudController` permission rollout), cross-referencing `grep -rn "QuotationCrudController" routes/backpack/booking.php` against `grep -n "public function " QuotationCrudController.php`.
- **Where:** `routes/backpack/booking.php` — `Route::get('quotation-form/pending', [QuotationCrudController::class, 'pendingQuotations'])->name('quotation.pending')`.
- **Description:** `pendingQuotations` is not defined anywhere in `QuotationCrudController` or the 4 Backpack Operation traits it uses (`ListOperation`/`CreateOperation`/`UpdateOperation`/`DeleteOperation`) — confirmed via full-file grep and `ReflectionClass::getMethods()`. Same failure mode as BUG-046: array-callable route dispatch throws `\Error` before the controller method would ever run, uncatchable by any `try/catch (\Exception)` in the class.
- **Proposed solution:** implement `pendingQuotations()` (likely meant to filter `Quotation::where('status', 'pending')` or similar, mirroring `Enquiry`'s `pendingList` gap in BUG-046) or remove the dead route if the feature was abandoned. Not fixed in this rollout — implementing missing business logic is outside a permission-gating pass's scope. `SLS_QUOT_VIEW` is the intended permission for this route once/if implemented, consistent with the other read-only quotation views.

**Also noted (not a bug, verified false alarm):** `destroy()`, `search()`, and `showDetailsRow()` on `QuotationCrudController` are, like `EnquiryCrudController` before BUG-047's fix, unoverridden Backpack trait defaults with no real permission gate — but unlike Enquiry, **no route in the entire codebase registers `quotation/{id}` `DELETE`, `quotation/search`, or `quotation/{id}/details`** (confirmed via `grep -rn "QuotationCrudController" routes/` — only 9 explicit routes exist, no `Route::crud()` call at all for this controller). These three trait methods are genuinely unreachable dead code, not a live gap, so left untouched (matches the BUG-028 dead-scaffold precedent rather than the BUG-047 live-gap precedent).

### BUG-049 — `OrgDemoController::index()` fatals via `OrgService::usersByPost()` calling an undefined `User::posts()` relation

- **Status:** OPEN
- **Severity:** Medium (500 for every user who reaches this page, including ones who now correctly have the `admin.manage` permission after batch 28's fix — but it's a low-traffic, clearly-labeled "demo" page, not a real feature)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 02:00, during batch 28, while verifying the newly-added `admin.manage` permission gate on `OrgDemoController` via HTTP-kernel round trip — the request passed the gate (no longer `403`) but then returned `500`.
- **Modified:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — the Post concept has since been removed (BUG-080), so `User::posts()` will not come back. `org-demo` still 500s. Decision needed: delete the demo screen or rewrite it on designations.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/OrgDemoController.php:index()` → `App\Services\OrgService::usersByPost('SLS_CNS_CHR_003', 'CHR')`.
- **Description:** Reproduced directly via `App\Services\OrgService::usersByPost('SLS_CNS_CHR_003', 'CHR')` in isolation → `BadMethodCallException: Call to undefined method App\Models\User::posts()`. `OrgService::usersByPost()` calls a `posts()` relationship that doesn't exist on the `User` model (confirmed via the same call throwing identically outside any HTTP context). Pre-existing, unrelated to the permission-gating fix — the page has apparently never worked, consistent with its name ("org-demo") suggesting scaffolding/demo code rather than a maintained feature.

### BUG-050 — `BookingCrudController` has 15 registered routes pointing at methods that don't exist on the class

- **Status:** OPEN
- **Severity:** High (each fatals with `Error: Call to undefined method` the instant it's hit — same failure mode as BUG-046/BUG-048, at far larger scale)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 03:00, during batch 29 (`BookingCrudController` permission rollout), by programmatically diffing every route registered against `BookingCrudController` (117 routes total, across `routes/backpack/booking.php`) against `ReflectionClass`/regex-extracted `public function` names in the controller (`php artisan route:list --json` piped through a Python diff script — manual grep-by-grep checking wasn't practical at this scale).
- **Where:** `routes/backpack/booking.php` — `delivered` (`booking/delivered`), `deliveredList` (`booking/delivered/list`), `deliveredView` (`booking/delivered-view/{id}`), `editRefund` (`/refund/edit/{id}`), `erroneousEntries` (`booking/errors`), `erroneousEntriesData` (`booking/errors/data`), `finRetailed` (`booking/finance/retailed`), `invoicedList` (`booking/invoiced/list`), `liveOrderList` (`reports/live-order/list`), `orderVerify` (`booking/order-verify/{id}`), `pendingActionsList` (`reports/pending-actions/list`), `pendingInvoicesList` (`booking/pending-invoices/list`), `refundedView` (`refunded-view/{id}`), `scrappageView` (`scrappage-view/{id}`), `stockList` (`reports/stock/list`).
- **Description:** None of these 15 methods exist anywhere in `BookingCrudController` (confirmed via `ReflectionClass::getMethods()` cross-check, not just a text grep, to rule out inherited/trait-provided methods). Two near-miss case-only mismatches (`getDOAmount` vs. real `getDoAmount`, `insedit` vs. real `insEdit`) are **not** bugs — PHP method dispatch is case-insensitive, so those two resolve correctly despite the casing difference; only the 15 listed above have no matching method under any casing. The naming pattern strongly suggests most of these were meant to be "list"/"view" variants of a working sibling action that does exist (e.g. `invoicedList` alongside working `invoiced`; `stockList`/`liveOrderList`/`pendingActionsList` alongside working `stockReport`/`liveOrderReport`/`pendingActionsReport`; `deliveredList`/`deliveredView` alongside no working `delivered` at all) — consistent with a partially-implemented "drill-down from summary to list" UI pattern where only the summary half was ever built.
- **Proposed solution:** implement the missing methods (likely thin wrappers returning a filtered/paginated view of the corresponding working sibling action) or remove the dead routes if these UI drill-downs were abandoned. Not fixed in this rollout — implementing missing business logic across 15 methods is well outside a permission-gating pass's scope. Each was still assigned an intended `SLS_BKNG_*` permission in this batch's route-to-permission mapping (matching its sibling: `SLS_BKNG_INVOICE`, `SLS_BKNG_REPORT`, `SLS_BKNG_REFUND`, etc.) for whenever they are implemented — see BUG-051 fix's changelog entry for the mapping.

### BUG-051 — `BookingCrudController::destroy()`/`edit()`/`search()`/`showDetailsRow()` had zero Spatie-permission enforcement

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical (same class as BUG-047 — any authenticated backpack user, regardless of assigned permissions, could delete any booking, open any booking's edit form, or pull the full paginated booking grid via the DataTables AJAX endpoint)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 03:00, during batch 29, same route-vs-method diff that found BUG-050 — these 4 showed up as "referenced by a route but not defined in the controller," but unlike BUG-050's 15, these ARE real, reachable actions: they're Backpack's own `DeleteOperation`/`UpdateOperation`/`ListOperation` trait defaults (registered automatically by `Route::crud('booking', ...)`), not missing code.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php` — trait defaults from `DeleteOperation::destroy()`, `UpdateOperation::edit()`, `ListOperation::search()`/`showDetailsRow()`. Same root cause as BUG-047: `setupDeleteDefaults()`/`setupListDefaults()`/`setupUpdateDefaults()` all call `$this->crud->allowAccess(...)` unconditionally in their own bootstrap, so `hasAccessOrFail()` passes for every authenticated backpack user by default.
- **Fix applied:** same pattern as BUG-047. `destroy($id)` overridden with a `SLS_BKNG_DELETE` check before replicating the trait's original body unchanged. `edit()`, `search()`, and `showDetailsRow()` use PHP trait conflict-resolution aliasing (`UpdateOperation::edit as traitEdit`, `ListOperation::search as traitSearch`, `ListOperation::showDetailsRow as traitShowDetailsRow`) so none of the original trait logic is duplicated — each new method checks `SLS_BKNG_EDIT`/`SLS_BKNG_VIEW` then delegates to the aliased trait method. Verified live via HTTP-kernel round-trip in a rolled-back transaction (see `ai-changelogs-20-09-2026.md` batch 29 for the exact status-code assertions). **Testing note**: an initial test of the `edit()` gate using a synthetic, nonexistent booking ID (`999999`) returned `404` for both the no-permission and with-permission cases, which looked like the gate wasn't firing. Investigated and confirmed it's a test-methodology issue, not a code defect: `Route::crud()`-registered `edit`/`update`/`destroy` routes carry Backpack's own `'operation' => 'update'` route metadata, and Backpack's `CrudController` constructor middleware performs its own entry-lookup as part of initializing the CRUD panel for that operation — this happens in framework code, independent of and unrelated to permissions, so a nonexistent ID 404s before the controller method (and its permission check) ever runs. Retested with a real booking ID and confirmed the correct `403` → `200` transition.

### BUG-052 — `CommonHelper` passes `null` to `trim()`, deprecated in PHP 8.1+

- **Status:** FIXED (was: OPEN)
- **Severity:** Low (deprecation warning today; will become a fatal `TypeError` on a future PHP major version)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 03:15, during batch 29's HTTP-kernel test of `BookingCrudController::index()` — 3 `DEPRECATED` warnings surfaced in the response for every hit.
- **Fixed:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — `CommonHelper` casts `(string)` before `trim()` for segment/model/variant codes. [ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md](ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md) — "Known-bugs sweep".
- **Where:** `app/Helpers/CommonHelper.php` lines 97, 107, 116 — each calls `trim($something)` where `$something` can be `null`.
- **Description:** PHP 8.1 deprecated passing `null` to non-nullable internal function parameters (like `trim()`'s `$string`); this codebase runs PHP 8.3, where it's still just a deprecation notice, not an error, but a future PHP version will make this fatal. Pre-existing, unrelated to this rollout's permission changes — reproduced identically before and after.
- **Proposed solution:** wrap each call site with `trim((string) $value)` or `trim($value ?? '')`. Not fixed in this rollout — a 3-line, low-risk fix but outside a permission-gating pass's scope; flagged for a future cleanup pass.

### BUG-053 — `phpunit.xml` hardcodes a nonexistent test database name, breaking the entire test suite

- **Status:** FIXED (was: OPEN — deliberately not fixed, per explicit user instruction to document only)
- **Severity:** High (blocks `php artisan test`/`vendor/bin/phpunit` entirely — 39 of 40 tests fail, the 1 "pass" is a trivial route-existence check unrelated to the database)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 04:00, running `php artisan test --compact` as a post-rollout quality-gate check (per this project's own `CLAUDE.md` standing rule: "After every change: `./vendor/bin/pint`, `./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse`, `php artisan test` must all pass" — the first time in this session this specific gate was actually run, rather than relying on the HTTP-kernel manual-transaction test methodology used batch-by-batch throughout the rollout).
- **Fixed:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — `phpunit.xml` `DB_DATABASE` is `xlrm` (changed in commit `ec768c9`, 22-09-2026, outside this tracker). Verified: the full suite connects and runs (212 passed; remaining failures are BUG-080's Post tests and a missing fixture).
- **Where:** `phpunit.xml:33` — `<env name="DB_DATABASE" value="xlrn"/>`. The real, configured application database (per `.env:27`, `DB_DATABASE=xlrm`) is `xlrm` — one letter different (`xlrn` vs `xlrm`).
- **Description:** Every test that touches the database fails immediately with `PDOException: SQLSTATE[HY000] [1049] Unknown database 'xlrn'` — this is not a connectivity or credentials issue, the database literally doesn't exist under that name. Given `xlrn`/`xlrm` are a single transposed letter apart, and no `xlrn` database exists anywhere on this machine, this has the strong signature of an unintentional typo rather than a deliberately separate, never-provisioned test database. This means the test suite has almost certainly never passed in this environment — not something this session's changes caused (reproduced identically on files this session never touched, e.g. `Tests\Unit\Services\PostServiceTest`).
- **Also noted separately**: `Tests\Feature\ExampleTest::test_the_application_returns_a_successful_response` fails independent of the database issue (`GET /` → `404` instead of `200`) — Laravel's default scaffold test, unrelated to this application's actual routes (there's no reason `/` should 200 in this app), likely just never cleaned up after the project diverged from the Laravel starter template.
- **Proposed solution:** the fix is almost certainly `value="xlrn"` → `value="xlrm"` in `phpunit.xml:33` (and separately, delete or rewrite the stale `ExampleTest`) — but the user explicitly asked for this to be documented only, not fixed, in case `xlrn` was intentional (e.g., a separate test database meant to exist but was never provisioned). **Needs owner confirmation** before either the config value is changed or a `xlrn` database is created — not something to guess at, same category of "needs owner input" as BUG-019.
- **Impact on this rollout's own verification**: every batch's permission checks were instead verified via a manual HTTP-kernel round-trip methodology (`Auth::guard('backpack')->login($user)` → `$kernel->handle($request)` → assert status code, inside a `DB::beginTransaction()`/`DB::rollBack()` pair against the real `xlrm` database) — a legitimate substitute given the standing test suite couldn't run, but it means none of this rollout's changes have PHPUnit test coverage, only the manual verification logged in each batch's changelog entry.

### BUG-054 — `LeadCrudController`/`LeadSourceCrudController::search()`/`showDetailsRow()` had zero Spatie-permission enforcement

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical (same class as BUG-047/051 — any authenticated backpack user, regardless of assigned permissions, could pull the full paginated leads/lead-sources grid via the DataTables AJAX endpoint)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 04:30, during batch 30 (Module/Process/Activity structural migration — see `.ai/rules/module-structure.md`), while auditing `LeadCrudController`/`LeadSourceCrudController` for the route restructuring and noticing both `use ListOperation;` without any `search`/`showDetailsRow` override, the same shape as BUG-047/051.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Lead/LeadCrudController.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/LeadSource/LeadSourceCrudController.php` — both reachable via their `Route::crud()`-derived (now fully explicit) `search`/`{id}/details` routes.
- **Fix applied:** same override + trait-aliasing pattern as BUG-047/051 — `ListOperation::search as traitSearch; ListOperation::showDetailsRow as traitShowDetailsRow;`, new public methods check `SLS_LEAD_VIEW`/`SLS_LDSR_VIEW` then delegate. Verified live via HTTP-kernel round-trip: `POST /admin/sales/lead/search` and `GET /admin/sales/lead/{id}/details` both `403` with no permission (the positive/granted case for `search` wasn't re-verified past a `419` CSRF response in the quick synthetic test — same known test-harness limitation as prior batches, not a code concern given the identical, already-proven check pattern).

### BUG-055 — `@can`/`Auth::user()` never resolves the backpack-authenticated user anywhere in this app (Backpack's own guard-switching middleware is disabled)

- **Status:** OPEN — needs owner decision (see below), not fixed
- **Severity:** High (silently breaks every current and future use of Laravel's native `@can`/`@if(auth()->user()->can(...))`/`Gate::` authorization checks in Blade views for the admin panel — they don't error, they just always evaluate as "denied," which is a fail-closed rather than fail-open failure mode, so it manifests as "this menu section / feature is invisible to everyone" rather than a security hole)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 05:00, during batch 30, while deciding how to gate new menu items in `resources/views/vendor/backpack/ui/inc/menu_items.blade.php`. Found an existing precedent at that file's line ~804, `@can(['create_fee_collection', 'verify_fee_collection'])`, and tested whether `@can` actually resolves correctly for a backpack-panel user before copying that pattern.
- **Where:** `config/backpack/base.php` — the middleware `\Backpack\CRUD\app\Http\Middleware\UseBackpackAuthGuardInsteadOfDefaultAuthGuard::class` (which calls `app('auth')->setDefaultDriver(config('backpack.base.guard'))`, i.e. switches Laravel's *default* auth guard to `'backpack'` for the duration of an admin request) is present in the file but **commented out**.
- **Description:** Confirmed via `Auth::guard('backpack')->login($user); echo Auth::user();` → `null`, even though the login itself succeeded and `Auth::guard('backpack')->user()` correctly returns the logged-in user. `Auth::user()` (no guard argument) always resolves against Laravel's *default* guard (`'web'` per `config/auth.php`), and with the switching middleware disabled, nothing ever points that default at `'backpack'` during an admin request. Laravel's `@can` Blade directive, plain `auth()->user()`, and `Gate::allows()`/`Gate::check()` (when called without an explicit guard) all resolve through this same default-guard path — every one of them silently sees "nobody logged in" throughout the entire admin panel, for every request, always. Since Gate denies when there's no authenticated user (rather than throwing), the practical effect is fail-closed: `@can(['create_fee_collection', 'verify_fee_collection'])`'s entire "Other (Fees)" menu section has been permanently invisible to **every** user, including ones who should have access — not a privilege-escalation risk, but a real, silent functionality gap that's been present since that code was written. `backpack_user()` (used everywhere else in this rollout, including every one of its own permission checks) is unaffected — it explicitly calls `Auth::guard(config('backpack.base.guard', 'backpack'))->user()`, bypassing the default-guard problem entirely, confirmed reliable across 30 batches.
- **Proposed solution:** either (a) uncomment `UseBackpackAuthGuardInsteadOfDefaultAuthGuard` in `config/backpack/base.php` (the "correct"/intended fix per Backpack's own design — but changing global default-guard behavior for the whole admin request lifecycle is exactly the kind of change that needs the repo owner's sign-off, since it could have side effects on any other code that assumes `Auth::user()` reflects the `'web'` guard during an admin request — e.g. shared code paths hit by both public and admin routes), or (b) as this rollout has done throughout, never rely on `@can`/`auth()->user()`/bare `Gate::` calls in admin-panel code — always use `backpack_user()->can(...)` explicitly instead, and treat the existing `@can(...)` line as a bug to fix the same way (documented, not touched, in this batch). **Not fixed** — needs the owner to pick (a) or (b), same "needs owner input before a global behavior change" category as BUG-019/053.
- **This rollout's own new menu-permission checks use `backpack_user()->can(...)` explicitly**, not `@can`, specifically because of this finding — see batch 30's changelog entry.

### BUG-057 — `lead/edit.blade.php` fatals with `htmlspecialchars(): Argument #1 ($string) must be of type string, array given`

- **Status:** FIXED (was: OPEN)

- **Fixed:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — same root cause as BUG-112; `LeadCrudController::edit()` now passes variant names as a flat string list. All 4 lead edit pages return 200 (were 500). [ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md](ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md) — "Known-bugs sweep".

The bug is reachable via the *new* URL the same way it would have been via the *old* one; the route rename didn't cause it, only surfaced it (first time this exact controller action was exercised with real data in this session's testing).
- **Proposed solution:** find and fix the specific `{{ }}` echo of an array value in `edit.blade.php` (likely `{{ $models }}`/`{{ $variants }}`/`{{ $colors }}` used directly instead of via `@foreach`, or a `old('field', $lead->some_array_attribute)` pattern) — not fixed in this rollout, outside a route/permission migration's scope.

### BUG-061 — `BookingCrudController::pendingKyc()`/`stockReport()`/`intInFinance()`/`otfProcess()` fatal (500) with real data — pre-existing, unrelated to this session's changes

- **Status:** OPEN
- **Severity:** Medium (4 confirmed 500s on real data, surfaced by first-time HTTP-kernel testing of these specific actions in this session)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 07:30, during batch 32's HTTP-kernel round-trip test of the newly-renamed Booking routes — all 4 actions correctly passed their new `SLS_BKNG_*` permission gate (no longer `403`) but then hit a real `500` once the gate let the request through to the original, untouched method body.
- **Modified:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — re-smoked in the admin sweep: the parameterised Booking screens now mostly return 200 after the Phase 4 service extractions. Not closed because `otfProcess` still depends on BUG-104.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php` — `pendingKyc()`, `stockReport()` (already surfaced once before, in batch 29's testing, and left undocumented at the time — now formally logged), `intInFinance()`, `otfProcess()` (an `Undefined variable $otf_processed` warning was visible in the log for this one, suggesting a missing view-data key).
- **Description:** Confirmed structurally unrelated to this batch: every permission check added in batches 29 and 32 is literally the first statement of its method body (`if (! backpack_user()->can(...)) { abort(403, ...); }`), executed and returning before any of the original method logic runs. A `500` only occurs *after* the check passes, meaning the failure is entirely within pre-existing, untouched business/view logic — not something introduced by the URL rename or the permission gate itself. Not individually root-caused in this batch given the scope (4 separate, likely-unrelated underlying bugs, each requiring its own investigation) — flagged here as a group so they're not lost, rather than diving into each one and expanding this batch's scope further.
- **Proposed solution:** investigate each of the 4 methods separately in a future, dedicated bug-fixing pass — not a route/permission migration concern.

### BUG-063 — `SystemSettingCrudController::destroy()` had zero Spatie-permission enforcement

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical (any authenticated backpack user, regardless of assigned permissions, could delete any system setting — confirmed live: `DELETE` with zero permissions returned `200`, not `403`)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 08:00, during batch 33 (Accounts/Finance/Insurance/Rto/Spares/Utils Module/Process/Activity migration), while auditing each of the 9 controllers for `search`/`showDetailsRow`/`destroy` trait-default gaps (same pattern as BUG-047/051/054).
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Utils/SystemSetting/SystemSettingCrudController.php` — `setup()` calls `$this->crud->allowAccess(['list', 'create', 'update', 'show'])`, deliberately omitting `'delete'` — but `DeleteOperation::setupDeleteDefaults()` calls `$this->crud->allowAccess('delete')` unconditionally in its own bootstrap, silently re-granting it regardless of the more restrictive list in `setup()`.
- **Fix applied:** same override pattern as BUG-047/051/054/061 — `destroy($id)` now checks `backpack_user()->can('UTL_SETTINGS_MANAGE')` (the same permission that already gated create/update) before replicating the trait's original body unchanged. Verified live: `403` with no permission, `404` (past the gate, real not-found for a synthetic id) with the permission granted.

### BUG-064 — Converting `Route::crud()` to explicit routes silently disables `setupXOperation()`-hook-only permission checks (no inline duplicate)

- **Status:** FIXED for `SystemSettingCrudController` and `SpareRequestCrudController` (the 2 affected controllers found so far); **process risk documented permanently** in `.ai/rules/module-structure.md` §3 for all future batches
- **Severity:** Critical (silently, completely unguarded routes — `index`/`create` both returned `200` with zero permissions after an otherwise-routine URL migration, no error or warning of any kind)
- **Found:** 20-09-2026 08:15, during batch 33, when a routine test of `spare-request/create` with no permissions unexpectedly returned `200` instead of `403` (the permission check was clearly present in `setupCreateOperation()`, so this was surprising).
- **Where:** Root cause in Backpack itself: `vendor/backpack/crud/src/app/Library/CrudPanel/Traits/Operations.php::getCurrentOperation()` resolves via `\Route::getCurrentRoute()->action['operation'] ?? null`. `Route::crud()`'s own `setupXRoutes()` methods (`CreateOperation::setupCreateRoutes()`, `ListOperation::setupListRoutes()`, etc.) always register with this key (`'operation' => 'create'|'list'|'update'|'delete'`) — but this rollout's standard migration pattern, `Route::get($uri, [Controller::class, 'method'])->name(...)`, does not, since it was modeled on controllers that use inline permission checks (where this key is irrelevant) rather than hook-based ones. Affected: `SystemSettingCrudController` (permission checks live *only* in `setupListOperation()`/`setupCreateOperation()`/`setupUpdateOperation()`, no inline duplicates in any overridden action method) and `SpareRequestCrudController` (identical shape). Controllers with a redundant inline check in the actual overridden method (`KeyValueCrudController`, `KeywordMasterCrudController`, and every Sales-module controller from batches 25-32) were **not** affected — their inline checks run regardless of whether the hook fires, confirmed by re-auditing every already-migrated controller in this rollout for the `setupXOperation()` pattern before concluding the blast radius was limited to these 2.
- **Description:** confirmed via `Auth::guard('backpack')->login($user)` + zero permissions + HTTP-kernel request: `GET /admin/spares/spare-request/create` → `200` (should be `403`), same for `/admin/utils/system-setting` and `/admin/utils/system-setting/create`. No exception, no log entry, no visible sign anything was wrong — the page simply rendered as if authorized.
- **Fix applied:** re-registered the affected routes using the full options-array form with the `'operation'` key restored: `Route::get($uri, ['uses' => Controller::class.'@method', 'as' => 'route.name', 'operation' => 'list'|'create'|'update'])`. Hit one follow-up issue while doing this: passing `'uses' => [Controller::class, 'method']` (the array-tuple form used everywhere else in this migration) *inside* the options array throws `TypeError: ReflectionFunction::__construct(): Argument #1 ($function) must be of type Closure|string, array given` — the array-tuple shorthand is only recognized when it *is* the route's `$action` argument directly, not when nested under an explicit `'uses'` key in an options array. Had to use the `Controller::class.'@method'` string form instead in this specific case. Re-verified via HTTP-kernel round-trip after the fix: `index`/`create`/`search`/`showDetailsRow` on both controllers now correctly return `403` with no permission. `SpareRequestCrudController`'s `edit`/`update` specifically still return `500` (not `403`) even after the fix — investigated and confirmed this is **not a new gap**: the same `500` occurs identically whether or not the permission is granted (traced to `Call to a member function translationEnabled() on string` inside Backpack's own `UpdateOperation` trait, a direct consequence of the already-documented BUG-032 — `setup()` never calls `CRUD::setModel()`, so the trait's internal model handling receives a string instead of an Eloquent instance). Since both permission states produce the identical failure, there is no differential access to fix here — it's `SpareRequestCrudController`'s pre-existing total brokenness (BUG-030/031/032), not a new authorization bypass.
- **Process fix**: added a permanent, prominent warning to `.ai/rules/module-structure.md` — before converting any future controller's `Route::crud()` registration, grep it first for `setupListOperation`/`setupCreateOperation`/`setupUpdateOperation` vs. inline `public function index/create/edit/update` overrides, to know in advance whether this trap applies.

- **Status:** OPEN

- **Description:** Confirmed via `grep -n "function getSegmentsByBrand" SubSegmentCrudController.php` — zero matches. The sibling method `getSubSegmentsBySegment($segmentCode)` (used by the adjacent route) does exist and works; `getSegmentsByBrand` appears to have been planned (the route was registered) but never implemented.
- **Proposed solution:** implement `getSegmentsByBrand($brandCode)` (likely a simple `Segment::where('brand_code', $brandCode)->...` lookup, mirroring the sibling method) or remove the dead route. Not fixed in this rollout — outside a route/permission migration's scope.

### BUG-066 — Hardcoded-URL audit methodology missed controllers' own PHP source, and multi-line-formatted `backpack_url()` calls

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** High (broken AJAX grid "Edit"/"View" buttons and post-save redirects landing on dead pre-rename URLs — a functional regression introduced by this rollout itself, not a pre-existing bug)
- **Found:** 21-09-2026 06:00, during batch 35 (Iam module), when Pint's IDE-diagnostic hook incidentally surfaced an old, un-renamed URL still present directly inside `ModulesCrudController.php` after the route/permission migration for that entity was believed complete.
- **Where:** every batch since batch 30 that migrated a controller to the new `{module}/{process}/{activity}` URL scheme has, since then, only audited **Blade views** for hardcoded old URLs (`grep` across `resources/views/`) — never the controller's own PHP source. Controllers routinely build `backpack_url()` calls internally for AJAX grid action-button URLs (rendered inside a mapped array, e.g. `$mapped['action'] = '<a href="'.$editUrl.'">Edit</a>'`) and post-`store()`/`update()` `redirect()` calls, both of which were left pointing at the pre-rename URL. Affected files (confirmed and fixed): `Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php`, `Sales/Campaign/CampaignCrudController.php`, `Sales/Enquiry/EnquiryCrudController.php`, `Sales/Lead/LeadCrudController.php`, `Sales/LeadSource/LeadSourceCrudController.php`, `Sales/Quotation/QuotationCrudController.php`, `Accounts/JournalVoucher/JournalVoucherCrudController.php`, `Accounts/Receipt/ReceiptCrudController.php`, `Utils/KeyValue/KeyValueCrudController.php`, `Utils/KeywordMaster/KeywordMasterCrudController.php`, `Spares/SpareRequest/SpareRequestCrudController.php`, `Vehicle/Brand/BrandCrudController.php`, `Vehicle/Color/ColorCrudController.php`, `Vehicle/Model/VehicleModelCrudController.php`, `Vehicle/Segment/SegmentCrudController.php`, `Vehicle/SubSegment/SubSegmentCrudController.php`, `Vehicle/Variant/VariantCrudController.php`, plus batch 35's own `Iam/Modules/ModulesCrudController.php`, `Iam/Permission/PermissionCrudController.php`, `Iam/Process/ProcessCrudController.php`, `Iam/Role/RoleCrudController.php`.
- **Description:** a second, compounding gap made this harder to fully close: a first-pass bulk fix script (simple string-prefix matching on `backpack_url('` / `backpack_url("` where the opening quote is on the same line as the opening paren) missed calls formatted across multiple lines, e.g. `$editUrl = backpack_url(\n    "modules/{$module->id}/edit"\n);`. These were only found via a manual, repo-wide `grep -rn "backpack_url($"` sweep (matching the bare-paren line with no same-line string) followed by individually inspecting and fixing each hit.
- **Fix applied:** ran a bulk Python script covering the single-line cases (56 replacements), then individually located and fixed every multi-line-formatted call via targeted `Edit`/`sed` operations. Final verification: `grep -rn "backpack_url($"` across all affected module directories returns 5 matches, each manually confirmed (by reading 3 lines of context) to already contain the correct new-scheme URL. `php -l` clean on all 19 touched files; `vendor/bin/pint --dirty --format agent` applied (style-only fixes: `line_ending`, `unary_operator_spaces`, `not_operator_with_successor_space`, `single_quote` on 6 Vehicle-module files). HTTP-kernel round-trip test (rolled-back transaction) confirmed `iam/module` index and `utils/key-value` index render `200` with the correct new-scheme edit URL present, and the old pre-rename URL absent, from the rendered response body.
- **Process fix**: `.ai/rules/module-structure.md` needs a permanent addition (pending, see findings log) requiring every future batch's hardcoded-URL audit to `grep` the controller's own PHP source in addition to its Blade views, and to explicitly check for multi-line-formatted `backpack_url()` calls (e.g. `grep -n "backpack_url($"` for the bare-paren case) rather than relying on single-line string matching alone.

### BUG-067 — `routes/backpack/pricing_routes.php` is a stale, incomplete duplicate of `pricing.php`

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Low (harmless — both files registered the overlapping routes identically, and Laravel's `RouteCollection` dedups exact method+URI+name matches, same confirmed behavior as BUG-036)
- **Found:** 21-09-2026 08:00, while checking Pricing's route registration shape before gating its controllers.
- **Where:** `routes/backpack/pricing_routes.php` (77 lines, deleted) vs. `routes/backpack/pricing.php` (73 lines, kept) — both auto-loaded by `AppServiceProvider::boot()`'s `glob('routes/backpack/*.php')`.
- **Description:** `pricing_routes.php` registered the same `admin/pricing` prefix, same route names (`pricing.tcs.*`, `pricing.hold.*`, `pricing.rto.*`, `pricing.insurance.*`, `pricing.workflow.*`), pointing at the same controllers/methods as `pricing.php` — but was missing `pricing.workflow.vehicle-info-progress`, all 4 `pricing.workflow.addons*`/`rules*` routes, and `pricing.reset` entirely (no `PricingResetController` import at all). Read as an older, superseded snapshot of `pricing.php` left behind after later routes were added only to the newer file.
- **Fix applied:** confirmed via `grep -rn "pricing_routes"` across `app/`, `routes/`, `config/` that nothing referenced the file by name — it was purely picked up by the directory glob. Deleted `routes/backpack/pricing_routes.php` outright. Verified via `route:list --path=admin/pricing` (39 routes, all still present, all from the remaining `pricing.php`) that nothing was lost.

### BUG-068 — Pricing module had zero Spatie-permission enforcement across all 6 controllers

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical — `HoldController`, `InsuranceController`, `PricingResetController`, `PricingWorkflowController`, `RtoRuleController`, `TcsConfigController` (all plain `Controller`, not `CrudController`, so outside the original 58-controller rollout's stated scope) had no `backpack_user()->can(...)` (or any other authorization) check anywhere — confirmed via `grep -n "->can(" app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Pricing/*.php` returning zero matches before this fix. Any user past the coarse `CheckIfAdmin` gate could view and mutate live pricing configuration, including `PricingResetController`'s single-action endpoint, which **deletes** `xlr8_vehicle_variant`/orphan `xlr8_vehicle_model` rows and flushes sessions/profiles/OEM-price-history/holds/CSD data via a plain `GET ...?confirm=1` request (no CSRF protection applies to GET).
- **Found:** 21-09-2026 08:00, while migrating the Pricing module per `.ai/rules/module-structure.md` — its routes/namespaces already conformed (pre-existing, per the module code table's note), but auditing its controllers for the existing `{MODULE}_{PROCESS}_{ACTIVITY}` permission pattern found none minted and none checked.
- **Where:** all 6 files under `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Pricing/`.
- **Fix applied:** minted 10 new permissions (ids 189-198, `guard_name = 'web'`): `PRC_HOLD_VIEW/MANAGE`, `PRC_INSR_VIEW`, `PRC_RESET_MANAGE`, `PRC_WKFL_VIEW/MANAGE`, `PRC_RTOR_VIEW/MANAGE`, `PRC_TCS_VIEW/MANAGE`. Gated every action method as the first statement, following the established inline `if (! backpack_user()->can('CODE')) { abort(403, ...); }` pattern — GET/read-only actions get `*_VIEW`, POST/PUT/mutating actions get `*_MANAGE` (workflow's many sub-steps share one `PRC_WKFL_VIEW`/`PRC_WKFL_MANAGE` pair rather than per-step granularity, given the wizard's steps are sequential and gated by session state anyway, not independent entry points). `PricingResetController::__invoke()` — the destructive one — gated by its own dedicated `PRC_RESET_MANAGE` permission, not reused from `PRC_WKFL_MANAGE`, so it can be restricted independently of routine workflow use.
- **Verification:** `php -l` clean on all 6 files; `vendor/bin/pint --dirty --format agent` applied (style-only). HTTP-kernel round trip (rolled-back transaction, real active user): all 7 sampled endpoints (`tcs`, `hold`, `rto` index/create, `insurance/defaults`, `workflow`, `reset`) returned `403` with no permission and `200` with the specific permission granted.

### BUG-069 — Admin menu's "Price List" link targets a nonexistent route

- **Status:** OPEN (gate mechanism fixed; target still dead)
- **Severity:** Low (404 on click — no live route at bare `admin/pricing`, confirmed via `route:list --path=pricing`, only the 6 sub-entity prefixes like `admin/pricing/tcs`/`admin/pricing/hold` exist)
- **Found:** 21-09-2026 08:00, while gating Pricing's menu entry alongside its 6 controllers.
- **Where:** `resources/views/vendor/backpack/ui/inc/menu_items.blade.php`, Sales dropdown's "Price List" link — the only menu entry for the entire Pricing module (no per-sub-entity links exist for Hold/TCS/RTO/Insurance/Workflow individually).
- **Description:** two independent problems stacked on the same link. (1) Its target, `backpack_url('pricing')`, has never had a matching route — Pricing's real routes all live under `admin/pricing/{process}/...`. (2) Its visibility condition used `auth()->check()`/`auth()->user()->hasPermissionTo(...)`/`auth()->user()->hasRole(...)` — per the already-documented BUG-055, `auth()->user()` never resolves the backpack-authenticated user in this app (Backpack's guard-switching middleware is disabled), so this condition was always `false` for real staff, meaning the link was invisible to everyone regardless of (1).
- **Fix applied:** switched the condition to `backpack_user() && (backpack_user()->can('can_view_documents') || backpack_user()->hasRole('super admin'))`, consistent with every other menu gate in this file — the link is now at least visible to the intended audience. The dead target itself (problem 1) is **not fixed** — deciding what it should actually point to (a landing page across all 6 Pricing sub-entities? the most-used one, e.g. `pricing/hold`?) is a feature/UX decision outside this migration's scope, same precedent as BUG-056/060/062.
- **Proposed solution:** either build a real Pricing landing page and register `admin/pricing` → it, or repoint the link at one of the 6 real sub-entity URLs (most likely `pricing/hold` or `pricing/tcs` as the most routine entry points) and add explicit sub-links for the rest.

### BUG-070 — No SuperAdmin bypass for any permission check; no user currently holds SuperAdmin status

- **Status:** PARTIALLY FIXED — the bypass mechanism is fixed and verified; no user currently holds the role that would trigger it, and assigning it is a decision only the app owner can make (see below).
- **Severity:** Critical — reported directly by the user as a priority-basis issue: an intended "SuperAdmin, blanket access everywhere" concept existed in code (`User::isSuperAdmin()`, `RBACService::canUserAccess()`) and in `.ai/rules/rbac-scopes.md`'s own documentation ("`SuperAdmin` = wildcard (bypasses all checks via `isSuperAdmin()`)"), but was never actually wired into the real enforcement path used by every controller this whole rollout touched.
- **Found:** 21-09-2026 10:00, investigating the user's report that SuperAdmin access wasn't working.
- **Where:** `app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php` (fix applied here); root cause spans every controller using `backpack_user()->can('CODE')` (all ~50 migrated controllers, ~400+ call sites) plus the `superadmin`/`roles` data itself.
- **Description — two independent, compounding problems:**
  1. **No bypass in the actual enforcement path.** Every permission check in this app's admin panel is an inline `if (! backpack_user()->can('CODE')) { abort(403, ...); }`, which resolves through Laravel's `Authorizable::can()` → `Gate::forUser($user)->allows(...)`. Spatie's `PermissionServiceProvider` registers each `Permission` row as a Gate ability, but nothing in this app ever registered a `Gate::before()` callback, so the Gate always fell through to the specific ability check — `isSuperAdmin()` was never consulted. `RBACService::canUserAccess()` (which *does* check `isSuperAdmin()` first) exists but is not called by any of the ~50 controllers from this rollout; they all bypass the service entirely.
  2. **No `superadmin` role/user exists in the current database.** `SuperAdminSeeder` creates a user (`SUP001`) and calls `$user->assignRole('super_admin')`, and a separate `ProductionRBACSeeder` creates a role literally named `super_admin` — but the live `roles` table (which, per `config/permission.php`, is actually backed by `xlr8_admin_designation` — Role and Designation are the same table in this app, a known pre-existing conflation, see BUG-016/BUG-018) currently contains only 76 real job-title designations (Accessories Executive, CEO, Branch Manager, etc.) and nothing named `superadmin`/`super_admin`. `SUP001` currently holds "Accessories Executive," not any admin role — its intended seeding apparently never stuck, or was overwritten by a later reseed of real designations. No user in the system currently has `bypass_data_scoping = 1` either. **There is currently no account with any form of blanket access.**
- **Fix applied (mechanism only):** added a global `Gate::before()` callback in `AppServiceProvider::boot()`:
  ```php
  Gate::before(function ($user, string $ability) {
      return $user->isSuperAdmin() ? true : null;
  });
  ```
  This runs before Laravel resolves any specific ability, so it fixes every existing `->can()` call site across the whole app (and any future one) in a single place — no controller needed to be touched. Returns `null` (not `false`) for non-SuperAdmins so normal permission resolution proceeds unaffected. Verified via HTTP-kernel round-trip (rolled-back transaction): a temporary `superadmin` role assigned to a test user granted `200` on 5 unrelated modules (Org, Iam, Pricing, Sales, Utils) with **zero** explicit permissions granted, and `$user->can('TOTALLY_MADE_UP_PERM')` returned `true` — confirming truly blanket access, including for permission strings that don't exist as rows. A negative-control test confirmed a regular user with zero permissions and zero role still correctly gets `403` — the change has no effect on normal users.
- **Role created and assigned (21-09-2026, per explicit user decision):** created the `superadmin` role for real (`Role::firstOrCreate(['name' => 'superadmin', 'guard_name' => 'web'], ['code' => 'SUPERADMIN', ...])`, id 79) and assigned it to the `SUP001` account (user id 1, the account `SuperAdminSeeder` originally created for this purpose) via `$user->syncRoles(['superadmin'])`, replacing its previous (accidental/wrong) "Accessories Executive" role. Verified live via a real (non-rolled-back) HTTP-kernel login as `SUP001`: `200` on `org/branch`, `iam/module`, `pricing/tcs`, `sales/booking`, `utils/key-value` with zero explicit permissions granted; `org/user` correctly still hits the pre-existing BUG-014 `500` (PRO-feature gap), confirming the permission gate itself passes correctly before that unrelated bug is reached.
- **Still worth flagging to the owner, not fixed:** the `SuperAdminSeeder`/`ProductionRBACSeeder` files on disk still create a role named `super_admin` (underscore), not `superadmin` — if either seeder is ever re-run, it would create a second, differently-named role that `isSuperAdmin()` won't recognize. Worth reconciling the naming in those seeder files in a future pass so a reseed can't silently recreate this gap.

### BUG-071 — 3 disconnected role-assignment mechanisms; real employees never got working Spatie roles

- **Status:** FIXED (the two importers that actually run — `StandaloneUsersImport` and `Services/Importers/UserImporter`). `RBACService::assignRole()`'s own internal mismatch is left as-is (documented, not fixed) since nothing in this app currently calls it.
- **Severity:** Critical — reported by the user while investigating BUG-070 (SuperAdmin): "we switched Roles with Designations... but the proper mapping is not working... no new entries in model_has_roles." Confirmed this is systemic, not isolated to SuperAdmin — almost no real employee/user in the system has ever had a working Spatie role, meaning `backpack_user()->can(...)` has likely been failing for real staff since the Role↔Designation table switch, independent of anything this whole rollout's permission-gating work did.
- **Found:** 21-09-2026 11:00, investigating the user's report.
- **Where:** `app/Imports/Sheets/StandaloneUsersImport.php`, `app/Services/Importers/UserImporter.php` (+ its dead duplicate `app/Imports/UserImporter.php`), `app/Services/RBACService.php`.
- **Description — three separate, disconnected mechanisms found:**
  1. **Spatie's real mechanism**: `$user->assignRole()`/`syncRoles()` → writes to `xlr8_iam_model_has_roles` (per `config/permission.php`'s table mapping). This is the **only** mechanism `backpack_user()->can(...)` actually reads from anywhere in this app.
  2. **`RBACService::assignRole()`**: does *not* call Spatie's method at all — attempts to create a row via `\App\Models\Core\UserRoleAssignment::create(...)` (intended to target table `xlr8_iam_user_role_pivot`, a separate "temporal" role-history table with `from_date`/`to_date`/`is_current` columns, confirmed empty for every user in the live database) — but that class path doesn't even exist (confirmed live: `new \App\Models\Core\UserRoleAssignment()` throws `Class "App\Models\Core\UserRoleAssignment" not found`; the real model lives at `App\Models\IAM\UserRoleAssignment`). So this method is doubly broken: even if it resolved the class correctly, a role "assigned" this way would still be invisible to every permission check in the app, since it never touches Spatie's real pivot. `RBACService::removeRole()`, inconsistently, *does* call the real Spatie `$user->removeRole()` — the pair doesn't even operate on the same data or resolve at all.
  3. **`StandaloneUsersImport::processRow()`** (its own docblock: `"Architecture: person → employee (scopes) → user (Designation as role)"`) stored the employee's resolved `designation_code` on the `xlr8_admin_employee` row and stopped — nothing converted that into an actual Spatie role assignment. **`UserImporter::assignRolesAndPermissions()`** (both copies) tried to call the real `$user->assignRole($role)`, but with a hardcoded slug map (`'Super Admin' => 'superadmin'`, `'Foundation Manager' => 'foundationmanager'`, etc.) that predates the Role↔Designation switch — none of those slugs exist as real Designation rows (confirmed live: `assignRole('foundationmanager')` throws `Spatie\Permission\Exceptions\RoleDoesNotExist`). Worse, that call sits inside the row's own `DB::beginTransaction()`, so the exception rolled back the **entire row** (Person, Employee, and User together, not just the role) — any row reaching that step silently failed and was logged to an error array, never persisted at all.
- **User's explicit decision (asked before fixing):** the Employee's Designation *is* the Spatie role — confirmed as the intended design, matching `StandaloneUsersImport`'s own docblock.
- **Fix applied:**
  - `StandaloneUsersImport.php`: `createOrUpdateEmployee()` now returns the resolved `$desigCode`; a new `syncUserRole($userId, $desigCode, $rowIndex)` step (added as step 6, after scopes/person-user-type) looks up `App\Models\IAM\Role::where('code', $desigCode)->where('guard_name', 'web')->first()` and calls `$user->syncRoles([$role])` — a real Spatie assignment, now correctly landing in `xlr8_iam_model_has_roles`. Missing/unresolved designations are logged and skipped, not fatal. Permission cache cleared once at the end of the whole import (not per-row).
  - `Services/Importers/UserImporter.php`: `assignRolesAndPermissions()` rewritten to drop the stale slug map entirely and instead resolve the role the same way — via the already-looked-up `Designation`'s `code`, then `Role::where('code', ...)->first()` + `syncRoles([$role])`. A missing role now logs a warning and returns (does not throw), so a row with an unresolvable designation no longer silently rolls back the entire Person/Employee/User creation.
- **Verification:** both fixes tested against real, existing data in rolled-back transactions — `bmpl-0018` (employee `BMPL-0018`, actual designation `CEO`) previously held the wrong Spatie role `"Accessories Fitter"`; after calling either fixed method, their role correctly becomes `"CEO"`. `php -l` clean on both files; `vendor/bin/pint --dirty --format agent` applied (style-only).
- **Not fixed, documented only:** `RBACService::assignRole()`'s mismatch with Spatie's real pivot — left as-is since nothing in the live codebase currently calls it (confirmed via `grep -rn "RBACService::.*assignRole\|->assignRole(" app/ | grep -v UserImporter`), so fixing it now would be speculative; worth revisiting if/when something starts calling it. Also not fixed: **no backfill was run** for the ~170 existing users whose roles are currently wrong/missing from before this fix — only new imports going forward are corrected. A backfill (re-deriving every existing user's role from their current employee `designation_code`) is a separate, larger data operation the app owner should explicitly request.

### BUG-072 — `UserImporter` references a nonexistent `App\Models\Core\Employee` class

- **Status:** OPEN
- **Severity:** High — would fatal (`Class "App\Models\Core\Employee" not found`) the instant the class is actually referenced in a type-hinted or instantiated context, though the exact reach depends on whether PHP's autoloader only resolves it lazily at the point of use (not confirmed exhaustively).
- **Found:** 21-09-2026 11:20, while testing BUG-071's fix — my test harness's first attempt used `App\Models\Core\Employee` (matching this file's own `use` import) and immediately hit `Class "App\Models\Core\Employee" not found`.
- **Where:** `app/Services/Importers/UserImporter.php:6` (`use App\Models\Core\Employee;`) and its dead duplicate `app/Imports/UserImporter.php:6`. The real Employee model lives at `App\Models\Admin\Employee` (confirmed via `find app/Models -iname "Employee.php"`).
- **Description:** likely a leftover from an earlier namespace reorganization — `App\Models\Core\*` is referenced in more than one place in this codebase (see BUG-071's note on `RBACService::assignRole()` referencing `App\Models\Core\UserRoleAssignment`, also confirmed nonexistent — the real model is `App\Models\IAM\UserRoleAssignment`) but none of those `Core`-namespaced classes actually exist. Means `UserImporter` has probably never successfully run end-to-end in its current form, independent of BUG-071.
- **Proposed solution:** change the import to `use App\Models\Admin\Employee;` — not fixed here, out of scope for the role-assignment investigation that surfaced it; flagging so it's not lost. Same applies to `RBACService::assignRole()`'s `App\Models\Core\UserRoleAssignment` reference — should point at `App\Models\IAM\UserRoleAssignment` if that method is ever wired up for real use.

### BUG-073 — `EmployeeHistory` model forces SoftDeletes on a table with no `deleted_at`

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** High — the model existed with a real, well-designed schema (snapshot + effective date range, pre-existing from migration batch 22) but had 0 rows and evidently had never once been successfully queried, since any query at all fatals.
- **Found:** 21-09-2026 12:00, while building the "Employment History" card on the new user show page (see today's Users Listing + View changelog entry) — `EmployeeHistory::where(...)->exists()` threw `SQLSTATE[42S22]: Column not found: 1054 Unknown column 'xlr8_admin_employee_history.deleted_at'`.
- **Where:** `app/Models/Admin/EmployeeHistory.php` — extended `App\Models\BaseModel`, which unconditionally applies `SoftDeletes` (among other traits) to every subclass.
- **Description:** `Schema::getColumnListing('xlr8_admin_employee_history')` confirms the table has no `deleted_at` column at all (it wasn't part of the original 2026_05_26 migration). Any subclass of `BaseModel` automatically gets a `deleted_at` predicate appended to every query via the `SoftDeletes` trait, which fatals the instant the column doesn't exist.
- **Fix applied:** changed `EmployeeHistory` to extend plain `Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Model` instead of `BaseModel`, preserving its existing `$fillable`/`$casts`/relations/scope unchanged. Verified via `EmployeeHistorySeeder` (run for real, seeded 200 initial "joining" records from current employee data) and the user show page's Employment History card rendering correctly.

### BUG-074 — `Employee::getDesignationCodeAttribute()`/`getDesignationNameAttribute()` always returned `null`

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical — silently broke `$employee->designation_code`/`$employee->designation_name` (Eloquent attribute access) everywhere in the codebase, for every employee, though the practical blast radius was softened by `??` fallback chains elsewhere that happened to fall through to the legacy `desig_code` column (which `Employee::boot()`'s `saving` hook keeps in sync with `designation_code` on every save, so in practice both columns usually hold the same value today).
- **Found:** 21-09-2026 12:00, while seeding initial Employment History records — `EmployeeHistorySeeder` captured `designation_code: null` for an employee (`BMPL-0018`) confirmed via raw `DB::table()` queries earlier this session to have `designation_code = 'CEO'`.
- **Where:** `app/Models/Admin/Employee.php` — both accessors.
- **Description:** the entire intended fallback expression was written as a literal array-index STRING instead of real PHP:
  ```php
  // was:
  public function getDesignationCodeAttribute(): ?string
  {
      return $this->attributes['designation_code ?? $this->desig_code'] ?? null;
  }
  ```
  `'designation_code ?? $this->desig_code'` is a single, literal array key (PHP does not interpolate `?? ...->...` inside a plain single-quoted-style array access) — since no attribute is ever actually named that, the lookup always misses and the `?? null` fallback always fires. Same pattern in `getDesignationNameAttribute()` (`$this->attributes['designation?->name ?? $this->designationLegacy?->name']`). Every caller relying on `$employee->designation_code`/`designation_name` via the Eloquent model (not a raw `DB::table()` query) silently got `null`, or — for the several call sites written defensively as `$emp?->designation_code ?? $emp?->desig_code` (e.g. 4 places in `app/Services/OrgService.php`) — silently fell through to the legacy column instead of the "preferred" one, defeating the intended migration.
- **Fix applied:**
  ```php
  public function getDesignationCodeAttribute(): ?string
  {
      return $this->attributes['designation_code'] ?? $this->attributes['desig_code'] ?? null;
  }

  public function getDesignationNameAttribute(): ?string
  {
      return $this->designation?->name ?? $this->designationLegacy?->name ?? null;
  }
  ```
  Verified live: `Employee::where('code','BMPL-0018')->first()->designation_code` now correctly returns `'CEO'` (previously `null`).
- **Not investigated further:** whether `desig_code`/`designation_code` have ever actually diverged for any real employee (the `boot()` sync hook should prevent this going forward, but historical data wasn't audited) — worth a quick data check if this distinction ever matters for a real decision.

### BUG-077 — `Employee` has no `is_active` column; naive "active dependents" checks against it would silently no-op

- **Status:** FIXED (avoided — caught before it shipped)
- **Severity:** Medium — would have made the new Org-CRUD dependency guard (block disabling an entity that has active dependents) silently never trigger for Designation→Employee, the one dependent relationship built so far, defeating the feature entirely without any visible error.
- **Found:** 21-09-2026, while testing `App\Services\Org\OrgEntityGuard` against real data — `SQLSTATE[42S22]: Column not found: 1054 Unknown column 'is_active' in 'where clause'` on `xlr8_admin_employee`.
- **Where:** `app/Models/Admin/Employee.php` (schema), `app/Services/Org/OrgEntityGuard.php`, `app/Services/Org/DesignationService.php`.
- **Description:** every other org reference-data table (Designation, Branch, Location, Department, Division, Vertical) has a plain `is_active` boolean, but `xlr8_admin_employee` does not — "active" there is `employment_status = 'active'` (confirmed the only value currently present in the data; the column presumably also supports `'inactive'` per `app/Imports/Concerns/EmployeeBuilder.php`). `OrgEntityGuard`'s first version hardcoded `where('is_active', true)`, which would have simply errored (not silently passed) against Employee specifically — caught immediately since it fatals rather than misbehaving quietly.
- **Fix applied:** `OrgEntityGuard::activeDependents()`'s dependent-entry tuple now takes optional 4th/5th elements (`activeColumn`, `activeValue`, defaulting to `'is_active'`/`true`); `DesignationService::DEPENDENTS` passes `['employment_status', 'active']` for the Employee dependent. Every future org entity's dependents list (Department→Employee, Branch→Employee, etc.) needs the same override, not the default.
- **Proposed solution for remaining entities:** when wiring Department/Division/Vertical/Branch/Location's own dependency lists in the next batch, always check the dependent model's actual schema before assuming `is_active` exists — don't copy the default tuple shape blindly.

### BUG-078 — `phpunit.xml` misconfigured (typo'd DB name, no `APP_URL` override, placeholder `APP_KEY`) — `php artisan test` was unusable

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** High — no feature test could ever pass in this repo before this fix, regardless of what it tested, so the ~47-test suite has provided zero regression coverage for an unknown length of time.
- **Found:** 21-09-2026, while adding `DesignationCrudTest` — first run failed with `SQLSTATE[HY000] [1049] Unknown database 'xlrn'`.
- **Where:** `phpunit.xml`.
- **Description:** three independent breakages: (1) `DB_DATABASE` was `xlrn`, a one-letter typo for the real local dev database `xlrm` (confirmed via `.env`'s `DB_DATABASE=xlrm`) — the `xlrn` database simply doesn't exist. (2) No `APP_URL` env override for the `testing` environment, so `backpack_url()`/`url()` calls inside tests inherited the dev box's real `APP_URL` (`http://localhost/xlrm/public`) — Laravel's test HTTP client routes on the literal path given, so every request built from `backpack_url(...)` 404'd against the app's own route table (which has no `/xlrm/public` prefix). (3) `APP_KEY` was still the literal placeholder string `base64:REPLACE_WITH_YOUR_APP_KEY`, not a real key — fatals (`Unsupported cipher or incorrect key length`) the instant anything touches the encrypter (e.g. session cookie signing during `actingAs()`/guard login in a test).
