- **Fix applied:** `DB_DATABASE` → `xlrm`; added `<env name="APP_URL" value="http://localhost"/>` (matches Laravel's own testing-environment convention); replaced the placeholder `APP_KEY` with a real generated key, scoped to the `testing` environment only (not the app's real key).
- **Verification:** `php artisan test --filter=DesignationCrudTest` → all 7 pass. Full suite now actually runs (see BUG-080 for what it surfaced once it could).

### BUG-079 — `actingAs($user, $guard)` breaks Spatie permission checks for any non-default guard in tests

- **Status:** WON'T FIX (framework/library interaction, not a bug in this codebase — worked around in tests instead)
- **Severity:** Medium — produces confusing false-403s in any test that logs in on the `backpack` guard and then checks a Spatie permission, which is the normal shape of every admin-panel authorization test this app will need going forward.
- **Found:** 21-09-2026, writing `DesignationCrudTest` — a user with `ORG_ENTITY_MANAGE` explicitly granted still got 403 from the real route, despite `$user->can(...)` returning `true` when checked before `actingAs()` was called.
- **Where:** test-only; `tests/Feature/Admin/Org/DesignationCrudTest.php`.
- **Description:** Laravel's `actingAs($user, $guard)` test helper calls `Auth::shouldUse($guard)` → `AuthManager::setDefaultDriver()` → overwrites `config('auth.defaults.guard')` for the rest of the test. Spatie Permission resolves a permission's guard from that same config value whenever none is passed explicitly (`Spatie\Permission\Guard::getDefaultName()`). After `actingAs($user, 'backpack')`, every `$user->can('X')` check looks for a permission with `guard_name = 'backpack'` — but all 233+ permissions in this app were seeded with `guard_name = 'web'`, so the check always misses. Confirmed this is test-only: Backpack ships a middleware (`UseBackpackAuthGuardInsteadOfDefaultAuthGuard`) that does the same `setDefaultDriver()` call for real requests, but it's commented out in `config/backpack/base.php` in this app, so real admin requests never touch `auth.defaults.guard` and stay on `'web'`.
- **Proposed solution:** don't use `actingAs($user, 'backpack')` in future tests. Log in directly on the guard instead: `$this->app['auth']->guard('backpack')->setUser($user);` (no `shouldUse` call — session persistence across the test client's simulated requests still works, confirmed via `assertAuthenticatedAs`). `DesignationCrudTest` has this as a private `actingAsBackpackUser()` helper; worth promoting to a shared `TestCase` trait once a second test file needs it, so it isn't rediscovered per file. Do **not** "fix" this by enabling `UseBackpackAuthGuardInsteadOfDefaultAuthGuard` — that would break every real `ORG_ENTITY_MANAGE`-style permission check app-wide, trading a test-only annoyance for a production outage.

### BUG-080 — 32 pre-existing test failures surfaced once BUG-078 was fixed (suite could not run before today)

- **Status:** OPEN
- **Severity:** Medium — none touch Designation/Org CRUD or anything else changed this session; flagged so they aren't mistaken for regressions from this branch, and so they're not lost now that they're finally visible.
- **Found:** 21-09-2026, first successful full `php artisan test` run after BUG-078's fix.
- **Where:**
  - `Tests\Unit\Services\ReportingServiceTest`, `Tests\Unit\Services\PostServiceTest` — `BindingResolutionException: Target class [App\Services\IAM\ReportingService] does not exist`.
  - `Tests\Unit\Models\EmpPostAssignmentTest`, `PostModelTest`, `PostReportingTest` — mix of `QueryException` (schema mismatch, likely missing/renamed tables) and `Error` (likely missing classes/methods).
  - `Tests\Unit\StandaloneUsersImportTest` — `FileNotFoundException: File storage/user_data.xlsx does not exist` (missing test fixture, not committed).
  - `Tests\Unit\RBACPersonEmployeeUserTest` — 1 failing assertion, not yet triaged.
- **Description:** all of these look like an abandoned or in-progress "Post" (position/reporting line) subsystem plus one missing test fixture — none were touched by, or related to, today's Designation CRUD work. `ReportingService`/`PostService` are bound as singletons in `AppServiceProvider` but the underlying class(es) appear to have moved, been renamed, or never existed.
- **Proposed solution:** needs its own investigation session — first confirm whether the "Post" subsystem is active/planned work or genuinely abandoned scaffold (similar to BUG-028's dead `FinanceCrudController`/`InsuranceCrudController` scaffolding) before deciding whether to fix, delete, or skip these tests.

### BUG-081 — `Vertical::employees()`/`employeeAssignments()` reference a nonexistent pivot table

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — dead/unreachable relations, worked around)
- **Severity:** Medium — both relation methods would fatal (`SQLSTATE... Base table or view not found`) the instant either is actually called; confirmed via `Schema::getColumnListing('xlr8_admin_emp_vertical_pivot')` returning an empty array (table doesn't exist), not an exception (MySQL/Laravel's `getColumnListing` degrades silently rather than throwing for a missing table, which is why this wasn't caught by a schema-listing sanity check alone).
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, while building `App\Services\Org\VerticalService`'s dependency guard (which entity actually represents "Employee assigned to this Vertical") — checked the model's own relations first, found `employeeAssignments()`/`employees()` pointing at `EmployeeVerticalAssignment` (table `xlr8_admin_emp_vertical_pivot`), then confirmed the table itself doesn't exist in the live schema.
- **Where:** `app/Models/Admin/Vertical.php` (`employeeAssignments()`, `employees()`), `app/Models/Admin/EmployeeVerticalAssignment.php` (`$table = 'xlr8_admin_emp_vertical_pivot'`).
- **Description:** the model's own docblock says "Employee pivot uses `vertical_code` → vertical's `code` column", suggesting the intended design was a pivot/many-to-many assignment table, but that table was apparently never migrated. The real, working link is the direct `employee.vertical_code` column (confirmed to exist and to be populated), which the Vertical model doesn't expose via any relation at all.
- **Proposed solution:** decide whether the pivot-table design is still wanted (and migrate `xlr8_admin_emp_vertical_pivot` for real) or whether the direct-column design (matching every other org entity's primary-assignment pattern — Designation, Department, Division) is the actual intended one, in which case `employeeAssignments()`/`employees()` should be replaced with a `hasMany(Employee::class, 'vertical_code', 'code')` and the dead pivot model deleted. Not decided or fixed here — `App\Services\Org\VerticalService`'s dependency guard uses the direct `vertical_code` column directly rather than either relation, sidestepping the question for now.

### BUG-082 — `Branch::primaryEmployees()` joins on a column that's never populated

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — dead relation, worked around in the new dependency guard)
- **Severity:** Medium — the relation is silently always empty rather than throwing, so it would look like "this branch has no employees" for every branch, always, without any error to surface the problem.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, while building `App\Services\Org\BranchService`'s dependency guard — checked `Branch::primaryEmployees(): HasMany(Employee::class, 'primary_branch_code', 'branch_code')`, then confirmed live that `xlr8_admin_branch.branch_code` is `NULL` for all 3 existing branches (`branch_code` is a real column but is absent from `Branch::$fillable`, so `Branch::create()`/`update()` never sets it), while 170 employees have a real `primary_branch_code` value (`BKN`, `CHR`) that matches `Branch.code`, not `Branch.branch_code`.
- **Where:** `app/Models/Admin/Branch.php::primaryEmployees()`.
- **Description:** `Branch::locations()` — right next to it — correctly joins `Location.branch_code` to `Branch.code` (its own comment claims `branch.branch_code` but the actual code uses `'code'`, and this is confirmed correct against real data: `Location.branch_code` values match `Branch.code` values). `primaryEmployees()` is the odd one out, joining to the wrong (always-NULL) column.
- **Proposed solution:** change `primaryEmployees()` to `hasMany(Employee::class, 'primary_branch_code', 'code')`, matching `locations()`'s pattern and the real data. Not fixed here — `App\Services\Org\BranchService`'s dependency guard uses `Employee.primary_branch_code` against `Branch.code` directly (via `OrgEntityGuard`, not this relation), sidestepping the broken relation rather than fixing the model.

### BUG-083 — `BranchCrudController`'s custom `setupListOperation()` shadows `ScopedCrud`'s data-scoping

- **Status:** OPEN (pre-existing — carried forward unchanged during today's Org CRUD refactor, not introduced or fixed)
- **Severity:** Medium — if branch-level data scoping is actually relied on anywhere (the `ScopedCrud` trait exists specifically to restrict which branches a non-superadmin user's list view shows), it currently has zero effect for the Branch list.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, while rewriting `BranchCrudController` to delegate to `BranchService` — noticed `use ScopedCrud;` is declared, and the trait defines its own `setupListOperation()` that calls `parent::setupListOperation()` then `$this->applyDataScope()`, but `BranchCrudController` **also** defines its own `setupListOperation()` (for the `ORG_ENTITY_MANAGE` permission check) — in PHP, a class's own method always wins over a trait's method of the same name, so the trait's version (and therefore `applyDataScope()`) never runs.
- **Modified:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — `ScopedCrud` itself now works (BUG-136). This controller still never calls `applyDataScope()`; turning it on is part of the open "enable data scoping" decision in BUG-136.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Org/Branch/BranchCrudController.php` (`setupListOperation()`), `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Traits/ScopedCrud.php`.
- **Description:** this shadowing existed identically before today's refactor (the pre-existing controller had the exact same pattern with `ORG_BRCH_VIEW` in place of `ORG_ENTITY_MANAGE`) — not a regression introduced by this session's changes, just carried forward as-is since fixing branch-level data scoping is a distinct, larger concern from the code-immutability/dependency-guard/media/permission work this phase covers.
- **Proposed solution:** either call `$this->applyDataScope()` explicitly at the end of `BranchCrudController::setupListOperation()`, or restructure `ScopedCrud` to hook in a way that doesn't require the consuming controller to remember to call it. Needs a decision from whoever owns the branch-scoping feature on whether it's still meant to be active before touching this.

### BUG-084 — `Location::branch()` and `Location::employeeAssignments()` both point at broken relations

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — dead relations, worked around in the controller and dependency guard)
- **Severity:** Medium — `$location->branch` silently always resolves to `null` for every location (same underlying cause as BUG-082); `employeeAssignments()` would fatal if ever actually called.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, while rewriting `LocationCrudController`/building `App\Services\Org\LocationService`.
- **Where:** `app/Models/Admin/Location.php` (`branch()`, `employeeAssignments()`).
- **Description:** two independent, already-logged defect patterns both land on this one model. `branch(): BelongsTo(Branch::class, 'branch_code', 'branch_code')` joins on `Branch.branch_code`, which is always `NULL` (same root cause as BUG-082 — `branch_code` isn't in `Branch::$fillable`) — confirmed live that `Location.branch_code` values (`BKN`, `CHR`, `SUJ`) actually match `Branch.code`, not `Branch.branch_code`, so this relation always returns `null` even though every location genuinely has a valid parent branch. `employeeAssignments(): HasMany(EmployeeLocationAssignment::class, 'location_code', 'code')` points at table `xlr8_admin_emp_location_pivot`, confirmed via `Schema::getColumnListing()` to not exist — same dead-pivot pattern as BUG-081 (Vertical) and the `xlr8_admin_emp_location_pivot`/`emp_vertical_pivot`/`emp_department_pivot`-style tables generally; worth checking whether ALL of this app's `emp_*_pivot` tables were ever actually migrated, not just these two.
- **Proposed solution:** fix `branch()` to join on `'code'` instead of `'branch_code'` (matching `Branch::locations()`'s already-correct reverse relation). For `employeeAssignments()`, same decision needed as BUG-081 — either migrate the pivot table for real or replace it with a direct `hasMany(Employee::class, 'primary_loc_code', 'code')`. Not fixed here — `LocationCrudController::index()` looks Branches up by `code` directly (`Branch::pluck('name', 'code')`) instead of via the relation, and `App\Services\Org\LocationService`'s dependency guard uses `Employee.primary_loc_code` directly rather than either broken relation.

### BUG-085 — Larastan OOMs on a full-project run in this dev environment

- **Status:** OPEN (environmental — not a code defect; scoped runs work)
- **Severity:** Low — doesn't block work, just means `vendor/bin/phpstan analyse` with no path argument can't be used as a blanket pre-commit check on this particular Windows dev box.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, verifying the newly-added "Development Workflow" rule (`CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`, added per explicit user request) actually works before relying on it — ran a full-project Larastan analysis to check.
- **Where:** environmental (Windows paging file size), not any specific file. Same family as BUG-034 (`composer dump-autoload` hanging).
- **Description:** `vendor/bin/phpstan analyse` (no path, default or `--memory-limit=1G`) fails with `VirtualAlloc() failed: [0x000005af] The paging file is too small for this operation to complete`, cascading into a misleading `Internal error: Class ... was not found while trying to analyse it` (a downstream symptom of the OOM, not a real missing-class bug).
- **Proposed solution:** scope every Larastan run to the specific directory/files just touched, with an explicit higher memory limit — confirmed working: `vendor/bin/phpstan analyse app/Services/Org --memory-limit=2G`. A real fix (increasing the Windows paging file size) is a machine-level change outside this repo's or an AI agent's scope — flag to the human operator if a full-project run is ever actually needed.

### BUG-086 — `makesPrimary()`/`makePrimary()` fatal on a specific promote-to-Primary sequence

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** High — silently broke the "promote a non-primary contact/address/bank account to Primary" action for a common, realistic case (a person with exactly one Primary + one Alternate/Secondary of the same type) — exactly the shape the new integrated Person screen's "Make Primary" button was built to drive.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, building the integrated Person edit screen (`org/person/{id}/edit`) — `POST org/person/{id}/contacts/{contactId}/primary` on an 'Alternate' Mobile contact 500'd while an existing 'Primary' Mobile contact was also present.
- **Where:** `app/Models/Admin/PersonContact.php::makesPrimary()`, `app/Models/Admin/PersonAddress.php::makePrimary()`, `app/Models/Admin/PersonBankingDetail.php::makePrimary()`.
- **Description:** all three columns (`contact_type`, `address_type`, `account_type`) are fixed-value MySQL `ENUM`s with a plain (non-deferred) unique index — `(person_code, data_type, contact_type)` for contacts, `(person_code, address_type)` for addresses, `(person_code, account_type)` for banking. Each `make(s)Primary()` method demoted the *old* Primary row to a fixed fallback value (`'Alternate'` for contacts/addresses, `'Secondary'` for banking) and then promoted `$this` to `'Primary'`, in two separate `UPDATE` statements. MySQL/InnoDB checks unique constraints per-statement, not deferred to end-of-transaction (unlike Postgres) — so whenever the row being promoted was *already* sitting in the exact slot the old Primary was about to be demoted into (e.g. promoting the one-and-only 'Alternate' Mobile contact while a 'Primary' Mobile contact exists), the demote statement tried to write a second row with the same `(person_code, data_type, contact_type)` tuple that the about-to-be-promoted row still held, and MySQL rejected it: `SQLSTATE[23000]: ... Duplicate entry 'PERS-000279-Mobile-Alternate' for key 'uq_person_contact_data_type'`. Reproduced live via `app()->handle()` HTTP round trip before touching the code, confirmed pre-existing (not introduced by today's session — `PersonContactCrudController`'s pre-existing standalone edit screen would have hit the exact same fatal on the same action, it just apparently was never exercised that way before).
- **Fix applied:** all three methods now run inside `DB::transaction()`; when the promoted row currently sits in the exact slot the old Primary would be demoted into, it's first staged through any other currently-unused valid value from that model's fixed type list (`CONTACT_TYPES`/`ADDRESS_TYPES`/`ACCOUNT_TYPES`) before the old Primary is demoted, then finally promoted to `'Primary'` — three writes instead of two only in the collision case, zero collisions possible. In the rare edge case where a person already has active rows in *every* type slot for that (person_code[, data_type]) combination (5/5 for contacts, 5/5 for addresses, 4/4 for banking), no free slot exists to stage through and the original two-statement sequence runs — same latent risk as before, but a genuinely rare shape (verified none of the seeded/import data hits it).
- **Verification:** `tests/Feature/Admin/Org/PersonCrudTest.php::test_promoting_an_alternate_contact_to_primary_demotes_the_old_primary` and `::test_adding_a_primary_address_and_making_a_second_one_primary` both exercise exactly this collision path through the real HTTP route and pass. Also manually verified the equivalent banking scenario in a rolled-back tinker transaction (Secondary → Primary while a Primary exists).

### BUG-087 — `Person::garages()` points at a nonexistent `App\Models\Admin\Garage`

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Medium — would fatal (`Class "App\Models\Admin\Garage" not found`) the instant the relation was actually called; the real `Garage` model lives at `App\Models\Core\Garage` (which already `use`s `App\Models\Admin\Person`, i.e. it was clearly written to relate to Person — just never wired up from the Person side).
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, running a scoped `vendor/bin/phpstan analyse app/Models/Admin/Person.php` (per the newly-added mandatory Larastan workflow step) while working on the integrated Person screen — Larastan flagged `Class App\Models\Admin\Garage not found` on the `garages()` relation.
- **Where:** `app/Models/Admin/Person.php::garages()`.
- **Description:** `hasMany(Garage::class)` with no `use App\Models\Core\Garage;` import resolves `Garage::class` to the current namespace (`App\Models\Admin\Garage`), which doesn't exist. `Garage::$fillable` includes a real `person_id` integer column, so Laravel's default `hasMany()` convention (foreign key `person_id`, local key `id`) is actually correct once the class resolves — this was purely a missing-import bug, not a wrong-relation-shape bug.
- **Fix applied:** added `use App\Models\Core\Garage;` to `Person.php`. Verified live: `(new Person())->garages()->getRelated()::class` now returns `App\Models\Core\Garage`; re-ran the scoped Larastan check and the `class.notFound` error is gone.

### BUG-088 — `StandaloneUsersImport` derives person codes independently of `Person::deriveCode()`, now inconsistent with it

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Low — both derivation orders produce a valid, real, unique code; the only issue is the two code paths (bulk import vs. the new integrated Person screen) now disagree on tie-break priority when a row has *both* a PAN and an Aadhaar number.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, while flipping `Person::deriveCode()`'s individual-entity priority to Aadhaar-first/PAN-second per explicit user instruction ("first choice is adhar, second is PAN") for the new integrated Person CRUD screen.
- **Modified:** 22-09-2026 — the Sales-system-refactor identifier-registry audit (Phase 1) found 3 *additional* importers with their own divergent `derivePersonCode()`/`resolvePersonCode()` implementations beyond the one this entry originally documented (`UsersImportSheet`, `EmployeeSheetImport`, `EmployeeRowDTO`), plus a live mobile-cleaning bug in 2 of them — see `docs/refactor/ai-findings-22-09-2026.md`.
- **Fixed:** 22-09-2026 — see `docs/refactor/ai-changelogs-22-09-2026.md` ("Phase 1 of Sales-system refactor: Identifier & Reference Registry").
- **Where:** `app/Imports/Sheets/StandaloneUsersImport.php`, `app/Imports/Sheets/UsersImportSheet.php`, `app/Imports/Sheets/EmployeeSheetImport.php`, `app/Imports/ValueObjects/EmployeeRowDTO.php` — all 4 had their own `derivePersonCode()`/`resolvePersonCode()`.
- **Description:** each import computed its own code with a private local method instead of delegating to `Person::deriveCode()` (the model-level SSOT). A row with both PAN and Aadhaar populated would get a different `person_code` depending on which of the 5 code paths (4 importers + the integrated Person UI) created it.
- **Fix applied:** all 4 importers now build a throwaway (unsaved) `Person` model instance from the row's `pan_no`/`aadhaar_no` and call `Person::deriveCode($person)` directly, instead of reimplementing the priority order and fallback shape. This also fixes the fallback-shape divergence (previously `PERS-<empcode>`, `EMP-<empcode>`, and a non-durable in-memory `PRSN#####` counter across the 4 files — all now `Person::deriveCode()`'s `PERS-######` durable sequence).

### BUG-089 — `SubSegment` model referenced a nonexistent `oem_name` column

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Medium — any code path selecting/filling `oem_name` on this model fatals (`Unknown column 'oem_name' in 'field list'`); the model's own `$fillable` and `generateCode()` both reference it.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, building the User onboarding screen's Vehicle card — `SubSegment::get(['code', 'oem_name', 'segment_code'])` 500'd on `org/user/create`.
- **Where:** `app/Models/Vehicle/SubSegment.php` (`$fillable`, `$columnTransformations`, `generateCode()`); confirmed via `Schema::getColumnListing('xlr8_vehicle_subsegment')` the real column is `name`.
- **Description:** likely a leftover from an earlier naming convention (the sibling `Segment` model does use `name` correctly) — `SubSegment` was never actually exercised with a real `oem_name` value before this session, since nothing previously selected that specific column by name.
- **Fix applied:** used the real column `name` in the new onboarding-screen queries (`app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Org/User/UserCrudController.php::onboardingLookupData()`) and the create/edit Blade views' JS. **Not fixed**: the `SubSegment` model itself still has `oem_name` in its `$fillable`/`columnTransformations`/`generateCode()` — out of scope for this task (would need verifying no other caller relies on the model accepting an `oem_name` key), flagged here so it isn't lost.
- **Proposed solution:** rename every `oem_name` reference inside `SubSegment.php` to `name`, then audit `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Vehicle/SubSegment/` (if it exists) and any importer that creates `SubSegment` rows for the same stale key.

### BUG-090 — 36 employees have an orphaned `designation_code`; 30 have no `primary_branch_code`

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — pre-existing data-quality issue, not introduced by this session)
- **Severity:** Medium — the new User edit screen correctly refuses to save an org/vehicle change for these employees until their org card is fully completed (designation/branch/location/department/division are all required once `user_type_code = emp`), which is the right behavior, but it does mean these 36+30 employees can't have an org/vehicle/permission change recorded via the new screen until someone fills in the missing primary fields first.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, while manually verifying `UserCrudController::update()` against real employees — the first two employees tried both had this problem (`designation_code = 'MAN'`, not a real row in `xlr8_admin_designation`; a separate employee with `primary_branch_code = ''`).
- **Where:** `xlr8_admin_employee` — confirmed via `Employee::whereNotNull('designation_code')->get()->filter(fn($e) => !Designation::where('code', $e->designation_code)->exists())->count()` → 36, and an empty-primary_branch_code count → 30.
- **Description:** these are real, pre-existing data gaps (either from historical migrations before the code-based org refactor, or from imports run before all designation codes existed) — not caused by anything in this session's Person/Org/User work.
- **Proposed solution:** a one-time data-cleanup pass (either backfill correct values from another source, or explicitly null them out with a documented reason) is needed before every employee can be edited through the new integrated User screen. Out of scope to guess at correct values here — needs the app owner's input on what the right designation/branch actually was for each of the 36+30 rows.

### BUG-091 — Booking Add/Edit rendered as an empty generic form after route restructuring

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical — Add/Edit Booking is a core Sales workflow; the form rendered with no fields at all (just a Cancel button), so no booking could be created or edited through the admin UI.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, user report with screenshots: "After your cleanup the booking Add/edit stopped working."
- **Modified:** —
- **Fixed:** 22-09-2026 — see `docs/refactor/ai-changelogs-22-09-2026.md` ("Fix: Booking Add/Edit rendering blank after route restructuring").
- **Where:** `routes/backpack/booking.php` (core CRUD block: `index`, `store`, `create`, `edit`, `update`, `destroy`, `search`, `showDetailsRow`, `show`).
- **Description:** commit `ec768c9` ("refactor: restructure admin permissions form requests and document changes") replaced `Route::crud('booking', 'BookingCrudController')` — which auto-registers every operation route with an `'operation'` route-meta key via Backpack's `CrudRouter::setupControllerRoutes()` — with 100+ hand-written `Route::get/post/put/delete(...)->name(...)` calls that never carried that key. `BookingCrudController::edit()`/`create()`/`search()`/`showDetailsRow()` all delegate to Backpack's own trait methods (`traitEdit()`, `traitCreate()`, etc.), which only invoke the controller's custom `setupUpdateOperation()`/`setupCreateOperation()`/`setupListOperation()` when the matched route carries that `'operation'` key (confirmed in `vendor/backpack/crud/src/app/Http/Controllers/Operations/{Create,Update,List,Show}Operation.php::setupXRoutes()`). Without it, `setupUpdateOperation()` never set `$this->crud->setEditView('admin.booking.add')` or built the local `$data` array of dropdowns/lookups, so Backpack fell back to its own generic single-hidden-field template — exactly matching the user's screenshot. Confirmed via `git show ec768c9 -- routes/backpack/booking.php` and a before/after HTTP round trip (`app()->handle()` against `/admin/sales/booking/{id}/edit`): before, response was Backpack's 144KB generic fallback; after, it's the real 500KB `admin.booking.add` form.
- **Proposed solution (applied):** restored the `['uses' => ..., 'as' => ..., 'operation' => 'list'|'create'|'update'|'delete'|'show']` keyed-array style (already the convention in `routes/backpack/core.php`) on exactly the 9 core CRUD routes that dispatch through Backpack's trait methods. The other 90+ custom Booking action routes in the same file were deliberately left as plain tuples — they call fully custom controller methods that don't go through `setupXOperation()`, so the key would be a no-op there. `QuotationCrudController` was checked and confirmed unaffected: its `index()`/`create()`/`edit()`/`update()` are fully self-contained (build their own data and `return view(...)` directly) and never delegate to Backpack's trait methods, so it never depended on the `'operation'` key.

### BUG-092 — Booking list's vehicle-detail columns (Segment/Model/Variant/Color) show N/A for almost all bookings

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — pre-existing data/design gap, not fixed here)
- **Severity:** Medium — cosmetic/reporting gap in the booking list grid, not a functional blocker; the underlying booking data isn't lost, it's just not resolvable through this particular join for records with no linked enquiry.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, investigating the same user report as BUG-091 (second screenshot showed N/A across the whole Segment/Model/Variant/Color columns).
- **Modified:** —
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php::getBaseQuery()` (selects `enq.segment_code`/`enq.model_code`/`enq.variant_code`/`enq.color_code` via a `leftJoin` to `xlr8_crm_enquiries` on `bookings.enq_no`) and `mapBookingForGrid()` (maps them straight through with `?? 'N/A'`).
- **Description:** `xlr8_booking_master` has no vehicle-detail columns of its own (confirmed via `Schema::getColumnListing`) — Segment/Model/Variant/Color are only ever available through the joined Enquiry row. `DB::table('xlr8_booking_master')->whereNull('enq_no')->orWhere('enq_no','')->count()` → 42 of 43 existing bookings, with `created_at` dates going back to at least 2026-06-03 — long before today's `ec768c9` restructuring, so this is **not** a regression from today's cleanup or from BUG-091, just a pre-existing consequence of how these bookings were created (without an enquiry link) colliding with a join-dependent list design. Ruled out routing as a factor: `index()`/`renderBookingListing()` are fully custom methods that never depended on Backpack's `setupListOperation()` dispatch, and the list page returned 200 both before and after the BUG-091 fix.
- **Proposed solution:** needs a product decision, not a code guess: either (a) backfill `enq_no` on legacy bookings that do have a resolvable enquiry, (b) have `xlr8_booking_master` store its own `segment_code`/`model_code`/`variant_code`/`color_code` directly at booking time instead of depending on the enquiry join (would need a migration + populating `store()`/`update()`), or (c) accept N/A as expected for bookings created without an enquiry and leave the design as-is.

### BUG-093 — 33 stale route names inside `BookingCrudController.php` throwing `RouteNotFoundException`

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical — several of these are core Booking pending/KYC/RTO/DMS/finance/exchange/insurance/refund/order-verification completion flows, and one is the FRS-documented "Quotation Required" prompt shown when a user opens OTF/VOTF on a Booking with no linked Quotation (a normal, expected alternate path per `docs/reference/Sales-Combined_FRS.md` §14).
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, auditing `BookingCrudController.php` against the newly-supplied Sales FRS — specifically checking the OTF mandatory-Quotation gate (`otfProcess()`), which led to `route('quotation.create', [...])` at (then) line 13344/13363.
- **Modified:** 22-09-2026 — a second, broader sweep of the same file (`grep -noP "route\(['\"]\K[a-z][a-z0-9_.\-]*"` against every `route()` call, not just ones starting with `booking.`/`quotation.`) found **14 more** stale call sites using an even older, differently-shaped naming scheme that never matched the `sales.booking.*` convention at all: `admin.booking.orderupdate` (×4, Order Verification accept/reject/hold/resume buttons), `dms-edit` (×2, unprefixed), `exchange-edit` (×3), `finance-edit` (×3, one inside dead commented code), `finance.retailedit`, `finance.payoutedit` (×2), `finance.view`, `insurance.edit`, `finance.do.edit`, `finance.retail`, `finance.payout`, `rejected.view`, `bookings.refunded` — bringing the total to 33 distinct broken `route()` call sites in this one file. Same root cause and same fix approach as the original 19; folded into this entry rather than opening a new bug ID.
- **Fixed:** 22-09-2026 — see `docs/refactor/ai-changelogs-22-09-2026.md` ("FRS-driven audit of the Sales process").
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php` — 33 distinct `route(...)` call sites (redirects, action-button `href`s, and the OTF quotation-missing JSON response) across Booking pending/KYC/DMS/RTO/finance/exchange/insurance/order-verification/refund/rejected workflows.
- **Description:** the module-structure route migration (`docs/refactor/TASK_STATE.md` batches 30-32) renamed every Booking/Quotation route to `sales.booking.*`/`sales.quotation.*`, and `.ai/rules/module-structure.md` §6 explicitly documents that every such rename must be paired with an audit of the controller's own PHP source for `route()`/`backpack_url()` calls (this is literally how BUG-066 was found and fixed on 20 other controllers in batch 35) — but all 33 call sites inside `BookingCrudController.php` itself were missed by that audit (or reintroduced by a later restructuring pass; not conclusively determined which). The second batch of 14 (`admin.booking.orderupdate`, `dms-edit`, `exchange-edit`, etc.) use a naming shape that doesn't match either the old `booking.*` or the new `sales.booking.*` convention, suggesting they may have predated even the original migration and been broken for longer. Confirmed live via `php artisan route:list --json` / direct `route($name, ...)` resolution in tinker: none of the 33 old names existed in the current route table before the fix.
- **Fix applied:** targeted `sed` substitutions scoped to this one file, mapping each stale literal to its confirmed-current `sales.booking.*`/`sales.quotation.*` equivalent (verified one-to-one against `php artisan route:list` output before applying, not guessed). Hand-verified via `git diff` that only the intended string literals changed. Swept the rest of `app/`, `resources/views/`, `routes/` for both the original and the second-batch naming patterns — the only remaining hits app-wide are 2 non-live stray backup files (`resources/views/admin/booking/otf-form.blade copy.php`, `resources/views/admin/quotation/copy of create with lines ui` — space-in-name, one has no `.blade.php` extension, neither resolvable as a real Laravel view), left untouched.
- **Verification:** live HTTP round trip — a Booking with no `quotation_id` hitting `sales/booking/otf-form/{id}` now returns `{"status":"quotation_missing",...,"quotation_url":"http://.../admin/sales/quotation/create?..."}` (200) instead of fataling. Direct `route($name, $params)` resolution in tinker confirmed all 33 fixed names now resolve to real URLs (e.g. `sales.booking.order-update` → `.../admin/sales/booking/order-update/1/2`). `grep -noP "route\(['\"]\K[a-z][a-z0-9_.\-]*"` over the file post-fix shows every remaining `route()` call starts with `sales.`.

### BUG-094 — `EnquiryCrudController::search()`/`showDetailsRow()` missing the `'operation'` route key

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Medium — `setupListOperation()` (which calls `setListView('admin.enquiry.list')`) never ran for these two AJAX-driven list actions, so they'd have rendered/behaved using Backpack's un-customized defaults instead of the app's own enquiry list view.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, same FRS-driven Sales-process audit — checked every trait-delegating method across `EnquiryCrudController`/`QuotationCrudController` for the BUG-064/BUG-091 pattern after finding it in Booking.
- **Modified:** —
- **Fixed:** 22-09-2026 — see `docs/refactor/ai-changelogs-22-09-2026.md`.
- **Where:** `routes/backpack/core.php` (`sales/enquiry/search`, `sales/enquiry/{id}/details`).
- **Description:** `EnquiryCrudController::search()`/`showDetailsRow()` both explicitly delegate to Backpack's own `ListOperation` trait methods (`$this->traitSearch()`/`$this->traitShowDetailsRow()`, aliased at the top of the class) after their own permission check — same shape as the already-fixed BUG-091 (Booking) and BUG-064 (SystemSetting/SpareRequest). Their routes were registered with plain `Route::post()`/`Route::get()` tuples, no `'operation'` key, so `setupListOperation()` never fired for these two specific actions (Enquiry's `index`/`create`/`store`/`edit`/`update`/`destroy` are all fully custom overrides with no trait delegation, so they were unaffected).
- **Fix applied:** converted both routes to the keyed-array form (`['uses' => ..., 'as' => ..., 'operation' => 'list']`), matching the established convention. Confirmed `QuotationCrudController` has no routes registered at all for its own `search`/`showDetailsRow`/`destroy` trait defaults (documented already in batch 27 of `TASK_STATE.md` as intentionally unreachable) — nothing to fix there.
- **Verification:** `sales/enquiry/{id}/details` returns 200 via HTTP round trip after the fix.

### BUG-095 — Hardcoded user-ID whitelists gate Order Verification / Pending DMS action buttons

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — not changed; looks like it could be deliberate but is undocumented and inconsistent with the rest of the app's access-control model)
- **Severity:** Medium — not a crash risk, but a real access-control inconsistency: every other gated action in this controller (and the whole Module/Process/Activity rollout, `.ai/rules/module-structure.md`) uses Spatie permissions (`SLS_BKNG_ORDER_VERIFY`, etc.); these three spots instead hardcode literal user IDs.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, during the FRS-driven Sales-process audit, while tracing the Order Verification/Pending DMS grids' action-button logic to fix BUG-093's stale routes.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php:4192` (`orderVerification()`), `:4352` (one of the pending-DMS/order listings — adds `$user->id` to the whitelist, so it's "these 3 fixed IDs, or yourself"), `:5051` (another DMS-related listing).
- **Description:** `$allowedUsers = [5, 23, 123]` (or `[5, 23, 123, $user->id]`) is checked with `in_array($user->id, $allowedUsers)` to decide whether a row shows its Accept/Reject/Hold/Resume/Process action buttons or just `---`. The method itself is already gated by a real permission (`SLS_BKNG_ORDER_VERIFY` etc.) as the first statement, so this is a *second*, narrower, undocumented gate layered on top — meaning a user who legitimately holds the permission but isn't one of these 3 IDs can view the list but never act on any row.
- **Proposed solution:** needs a decision from whoever owns this workflow — either (a) these 3 IDs represent a specific ops/admin team and should be replaced with a proper permission or role check (e.g. a dedicated `SLS_BKNG_ORDER_APPROVE` permission) so it's maintainable without editing code to add/remove a person, or (b) it's leftover debug/test scaffolding and the whitelist should be removed entirely in favor of the existing permission check. Not guessed at here — could silently lock out or unlock the wrong people.

### BUG-096 — Editing a `booked` Quotation silently reverted its status back to `raised`

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Medium — no data loss, but a documented-status-lifecycle violation (FRS §5.2/§9.1: `booked` is meant to stick once a Quotation converts to a Booking) with a real, visible side effect: `QuotationCrudController::index()` filters the main quotation list with `whereNotIn('status', ['booked'])`, so a `booked` quotation whose status got reverted to `raised` would reappear in the "needs action" list even though it's already linked to a real Booking.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, during the FRS-driven Sales-process audit — cross-checking `QuotationCrudController::update()` against E2E-BR-005/E2E-BR-016 (revision-on-material-change, `booked` status meaning) from `docs/reference/Sales-Combined_FRS.md`.
- **Modified:** —
- **Fixed:** 22-09-2026 — see `docs/refactor/ai-changelogs-22-09-2026.md`.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Quotation/QuotationCrudController.php::update()` (~line 1508 before the fix) — both the `Quotation::update(['status' => ...])` call and the paired `QuoteAction::create(['status' => ...])` call.
- **Description:** `update()` hardcoded `'status' => 'raised'` on every save, with no check of the quotation's current status. `edit()` has no guard preventing a `booked` quotation from being opened for edit, so any edit to an already-converted quotation (e.g. a typo fix) silently downgraded it back to `raised`. The revision-increment logic itself (`$hasQuotationChanges`) was already correct and unaffected.
- **Fix applied:** `$statusAfterUpdate = $quotation->status === 'booked' ? 'booked' : 'raised';`, used in place of the hardcoded literal in both the `Quotation::update()` call and the `QuoteAction::create()` call — preserves `booked` once set, otherwise behaves exactly as before. Deliberately did not add a hard block on editing a booked quotation (the FRS doesn't document one, and CONFLICT-003/GAP-002 already flag "what happens to Quotation status after Booking-side changes" as an open question needing business confirmation) — this fix only stops the *silent regression*, it doesn't add new restrictions.
- **Verification:** `php -l` clean, reviewed diff by hand (2-line logic change + one new local variable, no other lines touched). Could not run a live end-to-end HTTP round trip — this environment's `xlr8_crm_quotations` table currently has 0 rows, so there's no existing Quotation to convert to `booked` and re-edit through the real flow.

### BUG-097 — VOTF number generation and persistence are unlocked across two separate requests

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — needs a design decision, not a mechanical fix)
- **Severity:** Medium — a real but narrow race window (two users/tabs generating+saving an OTF for different bookings at nearly the same instant); not a routine every-request risk.
- **Found:** 22-09-2026, during the Sales-system-refactor Phase 1 audit, while evaluating whether to add `lockForUpdate()` to `generateVotfNumber()` to match `ReceiptCrudController::generateReceiptNumber()`'s concurrency safety (as originally scoped in the refactor plan).
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php::generateVotfNumber()` (scans every Booking's `final_data` JSON to compute the next branch/global sequence, returns it as JSON — no DB write) and `::otfSave()` (persists whatever `votf_no` value the client submitted, with no independent recomputation or uniqueness check).
- **Description:** unlike Receipt number generation (compute-and-write happen in one atomic action, correctly guarded with `lockForUpdate()`), VOTF generation is a **preview** AJAX call from one HTTP request, and the actual save happens in a **later, separate** request when the user submits the OTF form. A `lockForUpdate()` added to the preview endpoint alone would not close this race — the lock is released the instant that request finishes, long before the save request arrives. Two concurrent users generating a VOTF preview at nearly the same moment could both receive the same "next" number and both successfully save it.
- **Proposed solution:** needs a design decision, not a guess: (a) reserve the number at generate-time by writing a placeholder/pending row inside a transaction+lock, so a second concurrent generate can't compute the same "next" value, or (b) re-verify uniqueness of the submitted `votf_no` inside `otfSave()`'s own transaction before committing, rejecting (and prompting regeneration) on conflict. Not implemented here — flagging for the app owner to choose the approach before it's built.

### BUG-098 — `store()`'s XExchange creation set a nonexistent `vehicle_oem_code` column

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Medium — silently failed rather than crashing (caught by an existing try/catch and logged), but meant every "Exchange Buy" booking created through the main Booking form never actually got its `XExchange` row, breaking downstream exchange-verification workflows for those bookings with no visible error to the user.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, during the Sales-system-refactor Phase 3 pass (extracting `XExchange`'s duplicated "seed a new exchange entry" logic into a model method) — reproduced live in tinker before writing the shared method, confirmed `SQLSTATE[42S22]: Unknown column 'vehicle_oem_code'`.
- **Modified:** —
- **Fixed:** 23-09-2026 — see `docs/refactor/ai-changelogs-22-09-2026.md`.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php::store()` (the `XExchange` creation block for `buyertype === 'Exchange Buy'`).
- **Description:** the code did `$exchange->vehicle_oem_code = $booking->vehicle_oem_code;` before `save()` — `xlr8_booking_exchange` has no such column (confirmed via `Schema::getColumnListing`). Eloquent includes every set attribute in the generated INSERT regardless of whether a matching column exists, so the query itself failed at the database — caught by the surrounding `try/catch`, which logged the error and continued, leaving no `XExchange` row behind and no user-visible failure.
- **Fix applied:** extracted the correct field set into `XExchange::seedForBooking()` (see BUG-099 for the model method's full fix), dropping the invalid field entirely.

### BUG-099 — Neither `store()` nor `update()`'s XExchange creation set the required `vh_id` column

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** High — `update()`'s copy has no try/catch around it, so editing an existing booking to `buyer_type = 'Exchange Buy'` for the first time (no `XExchange` row yet) threw an uncaught `SQLSTATE[HY000]: General error: 1364 Field 'vh_id' doesn't have a default value`, a hard 500 on a core booking-edit action.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, same investigation as BUG-098 — reproduced live in tinker.
- **Modified:** —
- **Fixed:** 23-09-2026 — see `docs/refactor/ai-changelogs-22-09-2026.md`.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php::store()` and `::update()` (both `XExchange` creation blocks), and the new `app/Models/Module/Booking/XExchange.php::seedForBooking()`.
- **Description:** `xlr8_booking_exchange.vh_id` is `int NOT NULL` with no database default. Neither call site set it. `store()`'s failure was masked by its try/catch (same silent-failure shape as BUG-098); `update()`'s identical omission had no such guard and would throw straight through to a 500 response.
- **Fix applied:** `XExchange::seedForBooking()` now explicitly sets `'vh_id' => 0`, matching the sentinel value the codebase already uses elsewhere for "no vehicle chosen yet" on this exact column (`exchangeUpdate()`'s `'vh_id' => $request->enum_master1 ?? 0`). Both call sites now go through this one method.
- **Verification:** reproduced the original failure live in tinker (`SQLSTATE[HY000]: 1364 Field 'vh_id' doesn't have a default value`), confirmed the fix resolves it (`seedForBooking()` now succeeds and returns a real row with `vh_id = 0`).

### BUG-100 — `dmsupdate()` crashes when a DMS update clears every remaining pending item

- **Status:** FIXED (was: OPEN (documented only — needs a decision, not a mechanical fix))
- **Severity:** High — a hard, uncaught 500 on a routine, expected outcome of the DMS-completion workflow (finishing the last missing DMS field is exactly when this fires).
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, during the Sales-system-refactor Phase 4 pass (extracting `BookingDmsService`) — reproduced live in a unit test (`BookingDmsServiceTest::test_apply_with_zero_remaining_pending_items_hits_a_pre_existing_not_null_constraint_bug`).
- **Fixed:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — option (a): `BookingDmsService` stores `''` when nothing is pending. Chosen because the column is NOT NULL and existing rows already use `''` for "nothing pending"; no reader distinguishes null from `''`. Test updated to assert the save succeeds. [ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md](ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md) — "Known-bugs sweep".
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php::dmsupdate()` (now `app/Services/Sales/Booking/BookingDmsService.php::apply()`, same logic, same bug — extraction faithfully preserved it).
- **Description:** `xlr8_booking_master.pending_remark` is `NOT NULL` with no database default (confirmed via `SHOW COLUMNS`). The code does `$booking->pending_remark = !empty($finalPending) ? implode(...) : null;` — when a DMS update resolves the last remaining pending item, `$finalPending` becomes `[]`, so `pending_remark` is set to `null`, and the subsequent `saveQuietly()` throws `SQLSTATE[23000]: Column 'pending_remark' cannot be null`. This is a pre-existing bug in the original inline code, not introduced by the extraction — confirmed by inspecting `git blame`-equivalent history showing this exact ternary predates today's refactor.
- **Proposed solution:** needs a decision from whoever owns this workflow: (a) use `''` instead of `null` when clearing pending items (simplest, matches the column's actual constraint), or (b) migrate the column to nullable if `null` genuinely carries different meaning than `''` elsewhere that reads `pending_remark`. Not guessed at here since it touches a schema decision and possibly downstream code that reads this column.

### BUG-101 — `dmsupdate()`'s BEV/Personal "missing SO → order 3" branch is unreachable

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — long-standing dead branch, not caused by this refactor)
- **Severity:** Low — doesn't crash or corrupt data, but the code's own log message ("Order set to 3 - Personal/BEV from pending DMS, SO missing in this submit") describes a business rule that has likely never actually fired.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, same investigation as BUG-100 — a test asserting this branch's effect failed until traced to its root cause.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php::dmsupdate()` (now `BookingDmsService::apply()`/`isBevOrPersonal()`).
- **Description:** `xlr8_booking_master` has no `segment_code` column at all (confirmed via `Schema::getColumnListing`). `dmsedit()` (the GET form) explicitly resolves `segment_code` from the linked Enquiry and sets it on the in-memory `$booking` instance purely so *that request's* view can read it — this mutation is never persisted and doesn't survive to the next (separate) `dmsupdate()` request. `dmsupdate()` loads a fresh `Booking::findOrFail($id)` and never re-resolves `segment_code` from the Enquiry before checking `in_array($booking->segment_code ?? 0, [753, 21589])`, so that check is always comparing against `null` → always false → the `order = 3` branch never executes.
- **Proposed solution:** needs a decision: either (a) `dmsupdate()` should resolve `segment_code` from the linked Enquiry the same way `dmsedit()` does before this check (if the BEV/Personal SO-required business rule is still wanted), or (b) the branch and its associated `so_required` UI logic are genuinely obsolete and can be removed. Not guessed at here — could be intentionally simplified already, or could be a real gap in the DMS/SO workflow for BEV/Personal segment bookings.

### BUG-102 — `exchangeUpdate()`'s XExchange payload writes 9 fields that don't exist on the table

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — no data loss, cosmetic history-log noise only; a schema/scope decision, not a mechanical fix)
- **Severity:** Low — the same field values are correctly persisted onto the linked Enquiry row in the same request, so no customer/business data is actually lost. The only user-visible effect is `exchangeUpdate()`'s "changes" audit trail (written into booking history) reporting these 9 fields as changing on every single save, since the "old" value it reads back from `XExchange` is always `null`.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, during the Sales-system-refactor Phase 4 pass (extracting `BookingExchangeService`) — reproduced live in a unit test (`BookingExchangeServiceTest`, caught when an assertion on a saved `offered_price` came back `null`).
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php::exchangeUpdate()` (now `app/Services/Sales/Booking/BookingExchangeService.php::apply()`, same logic, same bug — extraction faithfully preserved it, did not attempt to fix).
- **Description:** `SHOW COLUMNS FROM xlr8_booking_exchange` confirms the table has only `id, bid, vh_id, purchase_type, verification_status, case_status, status, created_at, created_by, updated_at, updated_by, deleted_at, deleted_by`. `exchangeUpdate()`'s `$exchangePayload` array additionally sets `enum_master1`, `enum_master2`, `vehicle_details`, `vehicle_details2`, `registration_no`, `manufacturing_year`, `odometer_reading`, `expected_price`, `offered_price`, `exchange_bonus` — none of which exist as columns. `XExchange` (`BaseModel` + `HasColumnTransformations`) silently strips unknown attributes on mass-assignment (the same mechanism documented for BUG-098), so `XExchange::create()`/`->update()` both succeed but simply never write these 9 fields — no exception, no log, nothing to indicate the intended write didn't happen.
- **Proposed solution:** needs a decision from whoever owns the Exchange/Scrappage workflow: (a) add the 9 missing columns to `xlr8_booking_exchange` via a proper migration if this data genuinely needs to live on the exchange row (not just the Enquiry row) — e.g. for reporting/export queries that join only `xlr8_booking_exchange`; or (b) drop these 9 keys from `$exchangePayload` entirely as dead/aspirational code, since the Enquiry-side sync already captures the same data. Not guessed at here — touches a schema decision and possibly downstream reporting code that may (or may not) expect these columns to exist.

### BUG-103 — `requestRefund()`'s "Refund Requested Again" history branch never fires

- **Status:** FIXED (was: OPEN (documented only — an unambiguous one-line fix, but changes user-visible history output, so left as a product decision rather than silently applied))
- **Severity:** Low — no data corruption or crash; the refund request itself still succeeds and the booking still moves to "Refund Queued" (status 4) correctly. The only effect is a missing, more-specific history entry on the re-request-after-rejection path.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, during the Sales-system-refactor Phase 4 pass (extracting `BookingRefundService`) — noticed while tracing `$oldStatus`'s only read site.
- **Fixed:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — `BookingRefundService::apply()` captures the status before the update and checks `=== 7`. Applied as the unambiguous intent of the existing branch (the history text only makes sense for a re-request after rejection). 2 tests: re-request records the entry, first request doesn't. [ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md](ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md) — "Known-bugs sweep".
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Booking/BookingCrudController.php::requestRefund()` (now `app/Services/Sales/Booking/BookingRefundService.php::apply()`, same logic, same bug — extraction faithfully preserved it, did not attempt to fix).
- **Description:** The method captures `$oldStatus = $booking->status;` then immediately calls `$booking->update(['status' => 4, 'refund_request_date' => now()]);`, which also updates the in-memory `$booking->status` to `4`. The very next line checks `if ($booking->status == 7) { ...write 'Refund Requested Again' history... }` — but `$booking->status` is now `4`, never `7`, so this branch is unreachable. It was almost certainly meant to check `$oldStatus == 7` (was this a re-request after a prior rejection?).
- **Proposed solution:** change the condition to `if ($oldStatus == 7)`. Straightforward, but changes what appears in a booking's history log for this scenario going forward — flagged here for a product owner to confirm before applying, rather than silently changed during an otherwise behavior-preserving refactor.

### BUG-104 — otfSave()/store()/update() always crash: several Booking fields are not real columns

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — needs a schema/product decision before any fix)
- **Severity:** CRITICAL — confirmed to affect not just OTF Save but **booking creation (`store()`) and booking editing (`update()`) as well**. Every one of these three actions throws an uncaught 500-level `QueryException`. `store()`/`update()` are the two most fundamental actions in the entire Booking module — if this database's schema is what's actually running, no new booking can be created and no existing booking can be edited at all.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, during the Sales-system-refactor Phase 4 pass. First surfaced extracting `BookingOtfService` (`final_data` missing), then independently re-confirmed extracting `BookingCoreService` (`sale_type` missing) — both reproduced directly in tinker against real, pre-existing booking rows (id `1`), not just test fixtures, to rule out a test-only artifact.
- **Modified:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — re-verified: `xlr8_booking_master` still lacks `sale_type`, `final_data`, `consultant`, `buyer_type`, `accessories`, `segment_code`, `status_kw`. No migration in the repo defines this table, so the fix depends on the production schema. **Needs the owner's answer** (is production missing these too, or is the local DB stale?).
- **Where:**
  - `otfSave()` (now `BookingOtfService::apply()`): sets `$booking->final_data` unconditionally (plus `consultant`/`buyer_type`/`accessories` conditionally) then `save()`s.
  - `store()` (now `BookingCoreService::store()`): sets `$booking->sale_type` (among other fields) then `save()`s (an INSERT).
  - `update()` (now `BookingCoreService::update()`): sets `$booking->sale_type` (a `required`-validated field on the edit form, so it is *always* submitted and *always* differs from the always-null in-memory value) then `save()`s (an UPDATE) — reproduced live: `$b = Booking::find(1); $b->sale_type = '1'; $b->save();` throws `SQLSTATE[42S22]: Column not found: 1054 Unknown column 'sale_type'`.
  - Each extraction faithfully preserved this exact pre-existing logic; none of it was introduced or altered by the refactor.
- **Description:** `SHOW COLUMNS FROM xlr8_booking_master` (this session's `xlrm` local database) lists 50 columns. Missing from that list but read/written throughout `BookingCrudController`: `final_data`, `consultant`, `buyer_type`, `accessories`, `sale_type`, `branch_code`, `location_code`, `segment_code`, `model_code`, `variant_code`, `color_code`, `name`, `mobile`, `financier`, and others. Reading a missing attribute via Eloquent (`$booking->sale_type`) silently returns `null` (no error) — this is why the bug went unnoticed across 8 prior read-heavy Phase 4 extractions (KYC, DMS, Insurance, RTO, Delivery, Finance, Exchange, Refund all only *read* these fields for display, which degrades gracefully to `null`/`'N/A'`). Writing one of these fields via direct property assignment (which bypasses Eloquent's mass-assignment guard — confirmed via Laravel's `isGuardableColumn()`, which silently drops unknown-column keys only during `fill()`/`create()`/`update()`-the-array-method, not direct property sets) and then calling `->save()` builds a real `INSERT`/`UPDATE` statement against a column that doesn't exist, throwing `SQLSTATE[42S22]: Column not found`.
- **Scope now confirmed across all 9 Phase 4 services + Core CRUD:** the 8 non-OTF, non-Core services' `apply()`-style write methods were individually checked and only ever set real columns (confirmed via each service's own passing round-trip tests) — every one of those services' *read-side* `resolveEditData()`-equivalent methods mutate `$booking` with the same non-existent fields purely for that request's view rendering, and correctly never call `save()` afterward, which is why they were safe. `otfSave()`, `store()`, and `update()` are the only three Booking write paths in this controller that both set a non-existent field AND save — all three now confirmed broken, not just one.
- **Open question for the user/product owner, not guessed at here:** does this local `xlrm` database's schema reflect the actual production schema (in which case booking create/edit/OTF-save are live, critical, customer-facing bugs blocking the entire module), or is this local copy missing columns that a staging/production database already has (local-environment artifact, not yet a confirmed live bug)? This distinction changes the proposed solution significantly and needs a human decision — given the scope now includes the two most basic CRUD actions in the module, this is worth verifying urgently.
- **Proposed solution (pending the question above):** if confirmed live, add the missing columns via a proper Laravel migration, coordinated with whoever owns this table's schema outside Laravel's migration system (this table was not created by a Laravel migration in the first place — confirmed via `grep` finding zero migration files reference `final_data`, `sale_type`, or `branch_code`).

### BUG-107 — `admin.booking.edit.blade.php` is a completely orphaned view

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only — a pattern worth a dedicated cleanup pass, not a one-off fix)
- **Severity:** Low — no functional impact (nothing renders the file, so it can't misbehave in production), but it's a maintenance trap: this session's Phase 5 date-format work initially converted this file believing it was the live edit form, before discovering `admin.booking.add.blade.php` is the real one (via `CRUD::setEditView('admin.booking.add')`). Any future editor could make the same mistake.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, during the Sales-system-refactor Phase 5 pass (rolling out the centralized label registry) — a live HTTP round trip against `sales/booking/{id}/edit` showed none of the newly-added labels, tracing back to `setEditView()` pointing elsewhere.
- **Where:** `resources/views/admin/booking/edit.blade.php`; `BookingCrudController::edit()` (delegates to `traitEdit()`) and `setup()`'s `$this->crud->setEditView('admin.booking.add')` call.
- **Description:** `grep -rn "admin\.booking\.edit\b"` across the entire app (controllers, views, routes) returns zero matches outside the file itself. Combined with BUG-106 (`delivered-view.blade.php`, orphaned on both the route and view side) and the separately-noted dead `oldpendedit.blade.php` (a near-duplicate of the still-live `pendedit.blade.php`), this is the third orphaned Booking view found this session — suggesting `resources/views/admin/booking/` has accumulated dead files from past iterations that were never cleaned up when superseded.
- **Proposed solution:** a dedicated pass to grep every file in `resources/views/admin/booking/` against every `view('admin.booking.*')`/`setEditView`/`setListView`/etc. call in the controller, listing which views are genuinely reachable vs. orphaned, then a product decision on whether to delete the orphaned ones or keep them as reference/rollback material. Not attempted here — out of scope for the date-format/label work in progress when this was found.

### BUG-108 — OrgService branch-scoped user queries crash: missing pivot table

- **Status:** FIXED (was: OPEN (documented only — needs a product decision, not guessed at here))
- **Severity:** Medium — no current production impact found (every real Booking call site uses the default `'ALL'` branch scope), but the branch-scoping feature itself is completely non-functional for any caller that does pass a real branch code.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, during the Sales-system-refactor Phase 5 query-caching pass, while writing a unit test for the newly-cached `OrgService::salesConsultants()`/`usersByDepartment()` methods with a non-default branch code argument.
- **Fixed:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — branch filter in `userQuery()`/`usersByDesignation()` now uses `employee.primary_branch_code`, matching the dept/division filters beside it (which already use the employee's primary codes). Verified live: 6/3/123 users for branch BKN, no crash. If users should also match via secondary branch scopes (`user_scopes`), that's an extension, not a regression. [ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md](ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md) — "Known-bugs sweep".
- **Where:** `app/Services/OrgService.php::userQuery()` (the shared private helper behind `usersByDesignation()`, `usersByDepartment()`, `usersByDivision()`, `salesConsultants()`, `salesTeamUsers()`, `users()`).
- **Description:** The branch-scope filter does `$q->whereHas('branches', fn ($b) => $b->where('code', $filters['branch_code']))`, which Eloquent compiles into a join through `xlr8_admin_emp_branch_pivot` - a table that `SHOW TABLES` confirms doesn't exist in this database. Reproduced live: `OrgService::salesConsultants('SOME_BRANCH')` throws `SQLSTATE[42S02]`.
- **Proposed solution:** needs a decision from whoever owns the org/employee-branch data model: (a) the pivot table genuinely needs to exist and this is a missing migration, in which case branch-scoped user filtering is a real, currently-broken feature; or (b) branch scoping was superseded by a different mechanism (e.g. the `user_scopes` table already used elsewhere in this same file for non-primary scope matching) and this code path is dead and should be removed. Not guessed at here since it's a schema/architecture decision, not a mechanical fix.

### BUG-110 — Booking create/edit form's cascading dropdown AJAX calls all 404

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** High — user-reported live error. The entire segment→model→variant→color→accessories cascading dropdown chain, plus the branch→location dropdown, all silently failed on every booking create/edit form load, since every one of these 5 AJAX calls hit a nonexistent route.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, user-reported: "The route admin/get-models/BEV could not be found."
- **Where:** `resources/views/admin/booking/add.blade.php` (the real, live create/edit view - see BUG-107 for why `edit.blade.php` is a decoy).
- **Description:** 5 `$.ajax()` calls hardcoded URLs under a nonexistent `admin/get-*` path prefix (`admin/get-models/{segment}`, `admin/get-variants/{model}`, `admin/get-colors/{variant}`, `admin/get-accessories/{segment}/{model}/{variant}`, `admin/get-locations?type=sales`) instead of using Laravel's `route()` helper or the correct existing paths. The real, correctly-implemented equivalents already exist under the Booking module's own route group (`sales/booking/models/{segment_id}`, `.../variants/{model}`, `.../colors/{variant}`, `.../accessories/{segment}/{model}/{variant}`, `.../locations-by-branch/{branchCode?}`) - these were seemingly never updated when the stale URLs were introduced (or copy-pasted from an unrelated/older module). The 5th call additionally never included the selected branch code in its request at all (`this.value` was never referenced), so even pointed at the right route it would have returned all sales locations rather than the selected branch's.
- **Fix:** rewrote all 5 URLs to the correct `sales/booking/*` paths (matching each's actual named route's URI), and added the missing branch code to the 5th call so it correctly scopes to `getLocationsByBranch($branchCode)`. Verified: the exact originally-reported URL (`/admin/get-models/BEV`) still 404s (confirms the bug was real and specific), the new URL (`/admin/sales/booking/models/BEV`) returns 200, and all 5 endpoints were independently round-tripped with real segment/model/variant/branch codes from the database.

### BUG-111 — Dead duplicate `oldEnquiryCrudController.php` shadows real orphaned-view detection

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only)
- **Severity:** Low — dead code, not wired into any route, no functional impact.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, while scanning `resources/views/admin/enquiry/` for orphaned views during the Sales-module view-reorganization pass.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/oldEnquiryCrudController.php`.
- **Description:** A full duplicate of `EnquiryCrudController` — same class name `EnquiryCrudController`, but declared under namespace `App\Http\Controllers\Admin` instead of the real, live `App\Http\Controllers\Admin\Sales\Enquiry`. Confirmed via grep that nothing references this file's class or filename anywhere in `routes/` or `app/`, so it is dead code. It does, however, still contain `view('admin.enquiry.assigned-long-enquiry')`-style references to 4 of the 6 view files found orphaned in this same pass (`assigned-long-enquiry`, `assigned-quick-enquiry`, `unassigned-long-enquiry`, `unassigned-quick-enquiry`) — a future scan that greps `app/` indiscriminately (rather than the live controller only) would incorrectly conclude those views are still live.
- **Proposed solution:** Confirm with the code owner that this file holds no logic worth salvaging (a diff against the real controller would show what, if anything, diverged), then delete it. Out of scope for the current view-reorganization pass, which only touches views reachable from live controllers.

### BUG-112 — Lead edit screen 500s for any lead with a real vehicle selection

- **Status:** FIXED (was: OPEN (documented only))
- **Severity:** High — this is a live, user-facing crash on a core CRUD screen, not a cosmetic or dead-code issue.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, incidentally, while doing a live HTTP round trip to verify the Lead view move (`admin.lead.*` → `admin.sales.lead.*`) didn't break anything. Confirmed pre-existing (not caused by the move) by inspecting the moved file's content, which `git mv` leaves byte-identical.
- **Fixed:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — `LeadCrudController::edit()` passes `array_map(fn ($v) => $v['name'], OrgService::variants(...))`. All 4 lead edit pages return 200. [ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md](ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md) — "Known-bugs sweep".
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Lead/LeadCrudController.php::edit()` + `resources/views/admin/sales/lead/edit.blade.php`; root cause in `app/Services/OrgService.php::variants()`/`colors()`.
- **Description:** `edit()` passes `'variants' => OrgService::variants($lead->model_code)` and `'colors' => OrgService::colors($lead->variant_code)` into the edit view, which loops `@foreach ($variants as $code => $name) ... {{ $name }}`, expecting a flat `code => name` map — exactly what `OrgService::models()` correctly returns via `->pluck('name', 'code')->toArray()`. But `variants()`/`colors()` instead return `code => [array of fields: 'name', 'fuel_type_id', 'fuel_type', ...]` (a richer shape, presumably built for some other consumer). `{{ $name }}` on an array throws `htmlspecialchars(): Argument #1 ($string) must be of type string, array given`, a 500 for any lead whose `model_code` has at least one active variant. `LeadCrudController::create()` never hits this because it initializes `'models' => []`/`'colors' => []` unconditionally rather than eagerly resolving them.
- **Proposed solution:** Either have `edit()` map `variants`/`colors` down to `code => name` before passing to the view (e.g. `->map(fn($v) => $v['name'])`), or add a second flat-shaped accessor to `OrgService` (e.g. `variantNames()`/`colorNames()`) if other consumers rely on the richer array shape and can't be changed. Not fixed here — out of scope for the view-reorganization pass; flagged prominently given the severity.

### BUG-113 — Undefined-variable warnings on Vehicle SubSegment/Variant listing and create screens

- **Status:** FIXED (was: OPEN (documented only))
- **Severity:** Low — non-fatal PHP warnings, pages still render and return 200; not a crash.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, incidentally, while doing live HTTP round trips to verify the Vehicle module view move (`admin.{name}.*` -> `admin.vehicle.{name}.*`) didn't break anything. Confirmed pre-existing (not caused by the move) since the moved view content is byte-identical via `git mv`.
- **Fixed:** 24-09-2026 22:00 — `SubSegmentCrudController::create()` now passes `segments`; Variant create verified 200 in the same sweep. [ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md](ai-changelogs-24-09-2026.md) — "Known-bugs sweep".
- **Where:** `resources/views/admin/vehicle/sub-segment/list.blade.php` (via `SubSegmentCrudController::index()`, `$segments` undefined) and `resources/views/admin/vehicle/variant/list.blade.php` + `create.blade.php` (via `VariantCrudController::index()`/`create()`, an unnamed foreach target undefined/null).
- **Description:** `foreach ($segments as ...)` / a second unnamed foreach loop in these 3 views iterate over a variable never passed from their controller action, producing `Undefined variable` and `foreach() argument must be of type array|object, null given` warnings on every load. Since PHP warnings don't halt execution, the pages still render (200) with that section of the dropdown/list simply empty.
- **Proposed solution:** Trace what each foreach is meant to populate (likely a segment/variant filter dropdown) and have the corresponding controller action pass the missing array, or remove the dead markup if the feature was abandoned. Not fixed here — out of scope for the view-reorganization pass; flagged for a future UI/data-completeness pass.

### BUG-114 — BUG-110's original fix was itself still broken (wrong helper: `url()` vs `backpack_url()`)

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** Critical — the fix committed earlier this session for the user-reported live bug did not actually resolve it; all 6 Booking cascading-dropdown/receipt-check AJAX calls remained broken for any real browser user.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, during the deep-scan AJAX/JS re-verification pass explicitly requested by the user, while double-checking that BUG-110's fix actually worked end-to-end (not just that the target route resolved when hit directly).
- **Where:** `resources/views/admin/sales/booking/add.blade.php` (6 occurrences: models, variants, colors, accessories, locations-by-branch, check-receipt).
- **Description:** Laravel's `url()` helper only prepends `APP_URL` (here `http://localhost/xlrm/public`) to the given path — it has no awareness of Backpack's `admin` route prefix (`config('backpack.base.route_prefix')`, confirmed `'admin'`, applied via `Route::prefix()` in `routes/backpack/*.php`). BUG-110's fix used `{{ url('sales/booking/models') }}`, which generates `.../public/sales/booking/models` — a URL that does **not** match the real registered route (`admin/sales/booking/models/{segment_id}`). Verified directly: `Request::create('/sales/booking/models/BEV')` → 404; `Request::create('/admin/sales/booking/models/BEV')` → 200. The original fix was verified in this session's own earlier changelog entry by hand-typing the "/admin/..." path into the test request rather than rendering the actual Blade output and testing what URL it produces — a verification methodology gap that let this ship as "fixed" when it wasn't.
- **Fix:** replaced all 6 `url(...)` calls with `backpack_url(...)` (Backpack's own helper, confirmed via `backpack_url('sales/booking/models')` → `.../public/admin/sales/booking/models`, matching the real route). Re-verified every corrected endpoint via a live HTTP round trip with real segment/model/variant/branch/receipt-number values.
- **Process note:** this is a reminder that "the route resolves when I type the full admin-prefixed path into a test request" is not equivalent to "the Blade helper generates that exact path" — future AJAX-URL fixes in this app must render the actual Blade output (or use `backpack_url()`/`route()` from the start) rather than only testing route resolution in isolation.

### BUG-115 — 4 more Blade AJAX calls point at a wrong path (missing module segment)

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** High — 4 distinct live features affected across Booking, Vehicle SubSegment, and Vehicle Model.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, same deep-scan AJAX audit as BUG-114, by extracting every literal (non-`route()`/non-Blade-helper-only) URL string from all 38 Blade files containing AJAX calls and cross-checking each against `php artisan route:list`.
- **Where:**
  - `check-receipt`: `resources/views/admin/sales/booking/add.blade.php`, `amount.blade.php`, `pendedit.blade.php`, `recedit.blade.php` — all used `url('/admin/check-receipt')`, but the real route is `sales/booking/check-receipt/{rn}` (`BookingCrudController::CheckReceipt`).
  - `resources/views/admin/sales/booking/list.blade.php` (OTF-form link builder) — used `url('admin/booking/otf-form')`, real route is `sales/booking/otf-form/{id}`.
  - `resources/views/admin/vehicle/sub-segment/create.blade.php` — used `url('admin/sub-segment/segments')`, real route is `vehicle/sub-segment/segments/{brandCode}`.
  - `resources/views/admin/vehicle/vehicle-model/create.blade.php` + `edit.blade.php` — used `url('admin/vehicle-model/sub-segments')`, real route is `vehicle/model/sub-segments/{segmentCode}` (note: also `vehicle/model/...`, not `vehicle-model/...`).
- **Fix:** corrected all 7 occurrences to `backpack_url()` with the exact matching route path. Re-verified via live HTTP round trips (`check-receipt/RCP1` → 200, `get-do-amount` → 200, `vehicle/model/sub-segments/1` → 200). `vehicle/sub-segment/segments/1` still 500s after the fix — confirmed as the pre-existing, already-tracked BUG-010 (`getSegmentsByBrand` method never defined), not something this fix introduced or could resolve on its own.

### BUG-116 — Spares module's 3 AJAX-driven fields call routes/methods that were never implemented

- **Status:** OPEN (documented only)
- **Severity:** High — 3 distinct interactive fields on the Spare Request create/edit forms are completely non-functional for every user, not an edge case.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, same deep-scan AJAX audit as BUG-114/115.
- **Where:** `resources/views/admin/spare-request/create.blade.php` (all 3), `resources/views/admin/spare-request/edit.blade.php` (`get-variants` only); `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Spares/SpareRequest/SpareRequestCrudController.php`.
- **Description:** Three separate AJAX calls target `admin/fetch-parts`, `admin/check-ro-number`, and `admin/get-variants`. Confirmed via `php artisan route:list` and a full method listing of `SpareRequestCrudController` (`setup`, `destroy`, `fetchParts`, `data` — only 4 public methods total):
  - `fetchParts(Request $request)` **exists** as a method but has **zero route registered** anywhere in `routes/` pointing to it — calling it always 404s regardless of URL prefix.
  - `checkRoNumber`/`getVariants`-equivalent methods **do not exist at all** in this controller, and no route registers them either.
  This means the parts-autocomplete field, the RO-number-duplicate-check-on-blur, and the model→variant cascading dropdown on the Spare Request forms have never worked in this codebase (not something this session's reorganization broke).
- **Proposed solution:** needs a product decision, not a URL fix — either (a) implement the missing route for `fetchParts()` plus the 2 missing controller methods with real query logic (parts lookup, RO-number-exists check, model-scoped variant list), or (b) remove the dead UI/JS if these fields were abandoned mid-build. Left undone here since it requires design input on what each endpoint should actually query and return, not a mechanical URL correction like BUG-114/115.

### BUG-117 — Lead create always 500'd when a delivery date was entered

- **Status:** FIXED
- **Severity:** High — a core create-flow field, broken for every user who filled it in.
- **Found:** 23-09-2026, while extending the site_date()/DateFormatService rollout to the Lead module and investigating why `edit.blade.php`'s flatpickr used a hardcoded `d-m-Y` format instead of the site's configured one.
- **Where:** `app/Http/Controllers/Admin/Sales/Lead/LeadCrudController.php::store()`; `resources/views/admin/sales/lead/create.blade.php`.
- **Description:** `store()` parsed the submitted `expected_delivery_date` with `Carbon::createFromFormat('d-m-Y', $validated['expected_delivery_date'])`, uncaught.
